in the equality  $\mathbf{k} - \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n = \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top + \boldsymbol{\delta}$ , we view  $\boldsymbol{\delta}$  as the perturbation added to  $\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top$ . by weyl's inequality,

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\mathbf {k} - \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}) - \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top}) \right\| _ {\infty} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1),
$$

this proves (90) about the eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{k} - \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$ .

finally, from the kernel invertibility result (i.e. theorem 3.2), we have  $(1 + \eta)\mathbf{k} \succeq \mathbf{k}_n \succeq (1 - \eta)\mathbf{k}$  for some  $\eta = \check{\sigma}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$ . this implies

$$
(1 + \eta) \lambda_ {k} (\mathbf {k}) \geq \lambda_ {k} (\mathbf {k} _ {n}) \geq (1 - \eta) \lambda_ {k} (\mathbf {k}).
$$

so all eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{k}_n$  are up to a factor of  $1 + \check{\sigma}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$  compared with those of  $\mathbf{k}$ . this implies that the eigenvalue structure of  $\mathbf{k}_n$  is similar to that of  $\mathbf{k}$ .

lemma 6 indicates that the eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{k}$  and  $\mathbf{k}_n$  exhibit a group structure: roughly speaking, for every  $k = 0,1,\ldots ,\ell$ , there are  $b(d,\ell)$  eigenvalues centered around  $\gamma_{> \ell} + \gamma_k(k!)n / (d^k)$ . it is convenient to partition the eigenvectors according to such group structure.

we define

$$
\mathbf {u} = \left[ \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(0)}} _ {n \times 1}, \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(1)}} _ {n \times d}, \dots , \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {n \times b (d, \ell)} \right],
$$

$$
\mathbf {u} _ {n} = \left[ \underbrace {\mathbf {v} ^ {(0)} , \mathbf {v} ^ {(1)} , \ldots , \mathbf {v} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {n \times 1} \right]
$$

$$
\mathbf {d} = \mathrm {d i a g} \big (\underbrace {d _ {0} ^ {(0)}} _ {1 \times 1}, \underbrace {\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(1)}} _ {d \times d}, \ldots , \underbrace {\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {b (d, \ell) \times b (d, \ell)} \big),
$$

$$
\mathbf {d} _ {n} = \mathrm {d i a g} \big (\underbrace {d ^ {(0)} ,} _ {1 \times 1}, \underbrace {\mathbf {d} ^ {(1)} ,} _ {d \times d}, \ldots , \underbrace {\mathbf {d} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {b (d, \ell) \times b (d, \ell)} \big)
$$

we further define  $\mathbf{v}_0^{(\ell +1)},\mathbf{v}^{(\ell +1)}\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times (n - d)}$  and  $\mathbf{d}_0^{(\ell +1)},\mathbf{d}^{(\ell +1)}\in \mathbb{r}^{(n - d)\times (n - d)}$  such that they contain the remaining eigenvectors and eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{k}$ ,  $\mathbf{k}_n$ , respectively. we also denote groups of eigenvectors/eigenvalues of  $\psi_{\leq \ell}\pmb{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2\psi_{\leq \ell}^\top +\gamma_{> \ell}\mathbf{i}_n$  by

$$
\mathbf {u} _ {s} = \big [ \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(0)}} _ {n \times 1}, \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(1)}} _ {n \times d}, \ldots , \underbrace {\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {n \times b (d, \ell)} \big ],
$$

$$
\mathbf {d} _ {s} = \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\underbrace {d _ {s} ^ {(0)}} _ {1 \times 1}, \underbrace {\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(1)}} _ {d \times d}, \dots , \underbrace {\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(\ell)}} _ {b (d, \ell) \times b (d, \ell)}\right), \tag {94}
$$

and the remaining eigenvectors/eigenvalues are  $\mathbf{v}_s^{(\ell +1)}$  and  $\mathbf{d}_s^{(\ell +1)}$ . the following is a useful result about the kernel eigenvector structure.

lemma 7 (kernel eigenvector structure). let  $k \neq k' \in \{0,1,\dots,\ell + 1\}$ . denote  $\lambda_k = \gamma_{> \ell} + \gamma_k(k!) n / (d^k)$  for  $k = 0,\dots,\ell$  and  $\lambda_{\ell + 1} = \gamma_{> \ell}$ .

(a) suppose that  $\min \{\lambda_k / \lambda_{k'},\lambda_{k'} / \lambda_k\} \leq 1 / 4$  . then,

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1).
$$

(b) recall  $\pmb{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})}$  defined in (88). then,

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\left| \lambda_ {k} - \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \right| - \check {\sigma} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)}.
$$

proof of lemma 7. first, we prove a useful claim, which is a consequence of classical eigenvector perturbation results [dk70]. we will give a short proof for completeness. for a diagonal matrix  $\mathbf{d}$ , we denote by  $\mathcal{l}(\mathbf{d})$  the smallest interval that covers all diagonal entries in  $\mathbf{d}$ . if  $\mathcal{l}(\mathbf{d}_0^{(k')}) \cap \mathcal{l}(\mathbf{d}^{(k)}) = \emptyset$ , then

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}\right) \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min  _ {i , j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right|}. \tag {95}
$$

to prove this, we observe that

$$
(\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}) \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} + \mathbf {k v} ^ {(k)} = \mathbf {k} _ {n} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} = \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}.
$$

left-multiplying both sides by  $(\mathbf{v}_0^{(k')})^\top$  and re-arranging the equality, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} - \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}\right) \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} = \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} - \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {d} ^ {(k)} \\ = \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} (\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} - (\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

denote  $\mathbf{r} = (\mathbf{v}_0^{(k')})^\top \mathbf{v}^{(k)}$  for simplicity. without loss of generality, we assume  $\min_i(\mathbf{d}_0^{(k')})_{ii} > \max_j(\mathbf{d}^{(k)})_{jj}$ . let  $\pmb{u},\pmb{v}$  be the top left/right singular vector of  $\mathbf{r}$ . then,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} \mathbf {r} - \mathbf {r d} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \geq \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} \mathbf {r} \boldsymbol {v} - \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {r d} ^ {(k)} \boldsymbol {v} = \| \mathbf {r} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} \boldsymbol {u} - \| \mathbf {r} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} ^ {(k)} \boldsymbol {v} \\ \geq \| \mathbf {r} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \min  _ {i} (\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - \| \mathbf {r} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \max  _ {j} (\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this proves the claim (95). by the kernel eigenvalue structure (lemma 6),

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min  _ {i, j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right| = \left| \gamma_ {> \ell} + (1 + \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) (\lambda_ {k} - \gamma_ {> \ell}) - \gamma_ {> \ell} - (1 + \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) (\lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} - \gamma_ {> \ell}) \right| + \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \\ = \left| \left(1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \lambda_ {k} - \left(1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \right| + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \tag {96} \\ \end{array}
$$

by the assumptions on  $\lambda_{k}$  and  $\lambda_{k'}$ , with very high probability,

$$
\min  _ {i, j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right| \geq \max  \left\{\lambda_ {k}, \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \right\} / 2. \tag {97}
$$

by the kernel invertibility, theorem 3.2, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} (\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}) \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}) \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \cdot \left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \cdot \left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\mathbf{k}^{1 / 2}$  has the same eigenvectors as  $\mathbf{k}$ , we have

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {\left(k ^ {\prime}\right)}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \left[ \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {\left(k ^ {\prime}\right)} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {\left(k ^ {\prime}\right)}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (1 + \check {\partial} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}}} + \check {\partial} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \tag {98}
$$

similarly,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k}} + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \tag {99}
$$

we also note that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{\left[ \sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \right] ^ {2}} = \frac {1}{\lambda_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {k} _ {n} \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right)} \leq 1 + \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1).
$$

therefore, we deduce

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}\right) \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k} \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}}}. \tag {100}
$$

combining (97) and (100), we have shown that with very high probability,

$$
\big \| (\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \big \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\check {o} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k} \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}}}}{\max \{\lambda_ {k} , \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \} / 2} \leq \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1),
$$

which proves (a). now in order to show (b), we view  $\mathbf{k}$  as a perturbation of  $\psi_{\leq \ell} \lambda_{\leq \ell}^2 \psi_{\leq \ell}^\top + \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$ , cf. eq. (88). hence,

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min  _ {i , j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right|} \leq \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min  _ {i , j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right|}. \tag {101}
$$

(the first inequality is proved in the same way as (95).) by weyl's inequality,  $\max_j |(\mathbf{d}_0^{(k)})_{jj} - (\mathbf{d}_s^{(k)})_{jj}| \leq \left\| \boldsymbol{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})} \right\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . therefore, if  $\min_{i,j} |(\mathbf{d}_s^{(k')})_{ii} - (\mathbf{d}_s^{(k)})_{jj}| \geq 2\|\boldsymbol{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min _ {i , j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right| - \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min _ {i , j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right| - \min _ {i , j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right| / 2} \\ = \frac {2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min _ {i , j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right|}. \\ \end{array}
$$

if  $\min_{i,j}\left|\left(\mathbf{d}_s^{(k')}\right)_{ii} - \left(\mathbf{d}_s^{(k)}\right)_{jj}\right| < 2\|\pmb{\delta}^{\mathrm{(res)}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , then from a trivial bound we have

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 1 \leq \frac {2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min _ {i , j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right|}.
$$

as well. in either way,

$$
\left\| (\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\min _ {i , j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right|} \leq \frac {2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\left| \lambda_ {k} - \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \right| - \check {o} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)}
$$

we state and prove two useful lemmas. let  $\pmb{e}_1, \dots, \pmb{e}_n \in \mathbb{r}^n$  be the canonical basis. for an integer  $m \leq n$ , define the projection matrix  $\mathbf{p} = [e_1, \dots, e_m][e_1, \dots, e_m]^\top$ .

lemma 8. suppose that  $c'n \leq m \leq c'n$ , where  $c', c' \in (0,1)$  are constants. then there exists  $c > 0$  such that with very high probability,

$$
\sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p u} _ {s}) \geq c.
$$

proof of lemma 8. let the singular value decomposition of  $\psi_{\leq \ell}$  be  $\psi_{\leq \ell} = \mathbf{u}_{\psi}\mathbf{d}_{\psi}\mathbf{v}_{\psi}^{\top}$  where  $\mathbf{u}_{\psi} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}, \mathbf{d}_{\psi} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}, \mathbf{v}_{\psi} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$ . by the concentration result (51) we have  $\sigma_{\max}(\mathbf{d}_{\psi}) \leq (1 + \check{o}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1))\sqrt{n}$ . by definition,  $\psi_{\leq \ell}\mathbf{v}_{\psi} = \mathbf{u}_{\psi}\mathbf{d}_{\psi}$ . left-multiplying both sides by  $\mathbf{p}$  and rearranging, we have

$$
\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {\psi} = \mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \mathbf {v} _ {\psi} \mathbf {d} _ {\psi} ^ {- 1}.
$$

we use the concentration result (51) to  $\mathbf{p}\psi_{\leq \ell}$  (where we view  $m$  as the dimension) and get  $\sigma_{\min}(\mathbf{p}\psi_{\leq \ell}) \geq (1 - \check{\sigma}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1))\sqrt{m}$ . this then leads to

$$
\sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {\psi}) \geq \frac {\sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \mathbf {v} _ {\psi})}{\sigma_ {\max } (\mathbf {d} _ {\psi})} = \frac {\sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell})}{\sigma_ {\max } (\mathbf {d} _ {\psi})} \geq (1 - \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \sqrt {\frac {m}{n}}. \tag {102}
$$

so with very high probability,  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{pu}_{\psi}) \geq \sqrt{c'} / 2$ . since  $\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}$  and  $\boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top$  span the same column space, we can find an orthogonal matrix  $\mathbf{r} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  such that  $\mathbf{u}_s = \mathbf{u}_{\psi} \mathbf{r}$ . thus,  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{pu}_s) = \sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{pu}_{\psi}) \geq \sqrt{c'} / 2$  with very high probability.

lemma 9. suppose that  $\mathbf{u}_a, \mathbf{u}_b \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times k}$  are two matrices with orthonormal column vectors. let  $\mathbf{p} \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times m}$  be any orthogonal projection matrix, and  $\mathbf{p}_a^\perp = \mathbf{i}_m - \mathbf{u}_a \mathbf{u}_a^\top$ . then, we have

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p u} _ {b}) \geq \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p u} _ {a}) \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {u} _ {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {b}) - \sigma_ {\mathrm {m a x}} (\mathbf {p} _ {a} ^ {\bot} \mathbf {u} _ {b})
$$

proof of lemma 9. by the variational characterization of singular values and the triangle inequality,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {b}) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {b} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \geq \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\{\left\| \mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {a} \mathbf {u} _ {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {b} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| - \left\| \mathbf {p} \mathbf {p} _ {a} ^ {\perp} \mathbf {u} _ {b} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \right\} \\ \geq \sigma_ {\min } (\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {a}) \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \mathbf {u} _ {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {b} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| - \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \mathbf {p} _ {a} ^ {\perp} \mathbf {u} _ {b} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \\ \geq \sigma_ {\min} (\mathbf {p u} _ {a}) \sigma_ {\min} (\mathbf {u} _ {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} _ {b}) - \sigma_ {\max} (\mathbf {p} _ {a} ^ {\bot} \mathbf {u} _ {b}). \\ \end{array}
$$

this proves the lemma.

suppose  $n'$  is a positive integer that satisfies  $n \leq n' \leq (1 + c_2)n$  where  $c_2 > 0$  is a constant. denote  $n_0 = n + n'$ . let us sample  $n'$  new data  $\boldsymbol{x}_{n+1}, \ldots, \boldsymbol{x}_{n_0}$  which are i.i.d. and have the same distribution as  $\boldsymbol{x}_1$ . we introduce the augmented kernel matrix  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}} \in \mathbb{r}^{n_0 \times n_0}$  as follows. we define

$$
[ \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ] _ {i j} := \mathbf {k} _ {n} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) = \left( \begin{array}{l l} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} & \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \\ \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2 1} & \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2 2} \end{array} \right). \tag {103}
$$

where  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11},\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{12},\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{21},\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{22}$  have size  $n\times n$ $n\times n^{\prime}$ $n^{\prime}\times n$ $n^{\prime}\times n^{\prime}$  respectively. under this definition, clearly  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11} = \mathbf{k}_n$  . note that we can express  $\mathbf{k}_n^{(2)}$  as (recalling that  $\mathbf{k}_n(\cdot ,\pmb {x}) = [\mathbf{k}_n(\pmb {x}_i,\pmb {x})]_{i\leq n}\in$ $\mathbb{r}^n)$  ..

$$
\mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {(2)} = \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime}}} \left[ \mathbf {k} _ {n} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + i}) \mathbf {k} _ {n} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + i}) ^ {\top} \right] = \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2 1} ^ {\top} \right].
$$

so we can reduce our problem using  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}$ .

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {(2)} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {=} \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  follows from jensen's inequality, and  $(ii)$  is due to

$$
(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} = (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {2} + \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2 1}
$$

where  $(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}^2)_{11}$  denotes the top left  $n\times n$  matrix block of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}^2$ . similarly, we define the augmented vector

$$
\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} = \left(f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}\right)\right) _ {i \leq n _ {0}} = \left( \begin{array}{l} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {1} \\ \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \end{array} \right), \tag {104}
$$

and by jensen's inequality, we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} _ {n} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right\| \leq \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\|.
$$

hence, proving eqs. (67) and (68) is reduced to studying  $(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11})^{-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}^2)_{11}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11})^{-1}$  and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}^{-1}\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{12}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_2$  which can be analyzed by making use of the kernel eigenstructure.

lemma 10. there exist constant  $c_1, c_2 > 0$  such that the following holds. with very high probability,

$$
\left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}\right) _ {1 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c _ {1}, \tag {105}
$$

$$
\left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {n}. \tag {106}
$$

further, with high probability,

$$
\frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} \| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {c _ {2}}{n}, \tag {107}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} \| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| \leq \frac {c _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}}. \tag {108}
$$

consequently, we obtain (67) and (68) in lemma 3.

proof of lemma 10. step 1. first we prove (105) and (106). fix the constant  $\delta_0\coloneqq 1 / 4$ . we apply lemma 6 to the augmented matrix  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}$  (where  $n$  is replaced by  $n_0$ ) and find that its eigenvalues can be partitioned into  $\ell +2$  groups, with the first  $\ell +1$  groups corresponding to  $d$  eigenvalues. these groups, together with the group of the remaining eigenvalues, are centered around

$$
\gamma_ {0} n _ {0} + \gamma_ {> \ell}, \frac {\gamma_ {1} n _ {0}}{d} + \gamma_ {> \ell}, \dots , \frac {\gamma_ {1} (\ell !) n _ {0}}{d ^ {\ell}} + \gamma_ {> \ell}, \gamma_ {> \ell}. \tag {109}
$$

these  $\ell + 2$  values may be unordered and have small gaps (so that lemma 7 does not directly apply). so we order them in the descending order and denote the ordered values by  $\lambda_{(1)}, \ldots, \lambda_{(\ell + 2)}$ . let their corresponding group sizes be  $s_1, s_2, \ldots, s_{\ell + 2} \in \{1, b(d, 1), \ldots, b(d, \ell), n_0 - d\}$ . let  $\ell_0 \in \{1, \ldots, \ell + 1\}$  be the largest index  $k$  such that  $\lambda_{(k + 1)} / \lambda_{(k)} < \delta_0$ . (if such  $k$  does not exist, then  $\| \widetilde{\mathbf{k}} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq 2\gamma_{> \ell}(1 / \delta_0)^{\ell + 1}$  with very high probability, and thus (105) and (106) follow.) let the eigen-decomposition of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}$  be

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} = \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} + \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})}
$$

so that we only keep eigenvalues/eigenvectors in the largest  $\ell_0$  groups in  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^{\top}$ , and the remaining eigenvalues are in  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_1^{(\mathrm{res})}$  (in particular  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_1^{(\mathrm{res})}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}} = \mathbf{0}$ ). therefore

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} = \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\lambda_ {(1)} \mathbf {i} _ {s _ {1}}, \dots , \lambda_ {(\ell_ {0})} \mathbf {i} _ {s _ {\ell_ {0}}}\right) \cdot (1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)).
$$

and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_1^{(\mathrm{res})}$  satisfies

$$
\left(\gamma_ {> \ell} - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \mathbf {i} _ {n _ {0}} \preceq \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1} ^ {(\text {r e s})} \preceq \left(\gamma_ {> \ell} + c + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \mathbf {i} _ {n _ {0}} \tag {110}
$$

(where  $c > 0$  is a constant). taking the square, we have

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2} = \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} + \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})},
$$

where  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_2^{(\mathrm{res})}$  satisfies  $(\gamma_{> \ell}^2 - \check{o}_{d, \mathbb{p}}(1)) \mathbf{i}_{2n} \preceq \widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_2^{(\mathrm{res})} \preceq ((\gamma_{> \ell} + c)^2 + \check{o}_{d, \mathbb{p}}(1)) \mathbf{i}_{2n}$ .

for convenience, we denote  $d_0$  to be the size of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}$  (i.e., number of eigenvalues in the main part of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}$ ). also denote by  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1 \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$ ,  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_2 \in \mathbb{r}^{n' \times d}$  the submatrix of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}$  formed by its top- $n$ /bottom- $n'$  rows respectively. using this eigen-decomposition, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}\right) _ {1 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} (111) \\ + \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {2} ^ {(\text {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + 1. (112) \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}$  (or equivalently  $\mathbf{k}_n$ ) has smallest eigenvalue bounded away from zero with very high probability by theorem 3.2, we can see that the two terms in the second line (112) are bounded with very high probability. to handle the term on the right-hand side of (111), we use the identity

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} = \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} - \mathbf {a} _ {0}) \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} - \mathbf {a} _ {0}) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \\ + \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} - \mathbf {a} _ {0}) \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} - \mathbf {a} _ {0}) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \tag {113} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathbf{a}_0, \mathbf{a}$  are nonsingular symmetric matrices and  $\mathbf{h}$  is a symmetric matrix. define  $\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_{\gamma} = \widetilde{\mathbf{d}} - \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_{d}$ , which is strictly positive definite with very high probability (since by definition,  $\lambda_{(\ell_0)} \geq \delta_0^{-1} \gamma_{> \ell}$ ). we set  $\mathbf{a} = \widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}, \mathbf{a}_0 = [\widetilde{\mathbf{u}} \widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_{\lambda} \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^\top]_{11} + \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$ , and  $\mathbf{h} = [\widetilde{\mathbf{u}} \widetilde{\mathbf{d}}^2 \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}^\top]_{11}$ , and it holds that  $\| \mathbf{a}^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$ ,  $\| \mathbf{a}_0^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$  and  $\| \mathbf{a} - \mathbf{a}_0 \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$  with very high probability by the above. therefore, with very high probability,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq c \cdot \left\| \left([ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} ] _ {1 1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} [ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} ] _ {1 1} \left([ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} ] _ {1 1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ = c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \left(\gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {2} \mathbf {i} _ {d}\right) \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ + c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {=} c + c \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \big (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \big) ^ {- 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i i i)} {\leq} c + c \cdot \left[ \lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}\right) \right] ^ {- 1} \tag {114} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  is because  $\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}^2\preceq 2\gamma_{> \ell}^2\mathbf{i}_d + 2(\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_\gamma)^2$ ,  $(ii)$  is because by the identity (156) in lemma 23 we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} = \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} ^ {1 / 2} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {d _ {0}}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \big (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} ^ {- 1} \big) ^ {- 1}; \\ \end{array}
$$

and (iii) is due to  $\| \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \| \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = 1$

similarly, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \leq \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| + \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1} ^ {(\text {r e s})}\right) _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \\ \leq \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {2} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {2} \right\| + \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {f}} _ {2} \right\|. \\ \end{array}
$$

it is clear that the second term on the last line is bounded by  $c\| \widetilde{\boldsymbol{f}}_2\|$  with very high probability, by eq. (110). for the first term, with very high probability,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \leq c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \left(\gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {d}\right) \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {2} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| (115) \\ + c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {2} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \\ \leq c \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| + c \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {2} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \\ \leq c \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| + c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| \\ \leq c \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| + c \cdot \left[ \lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}\right) \right] ^ {- 1} \cdot \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \|. (116) \\ \end{array}
$$

by  $\| f \|_{l^2} < c$  and the law of large numbers,  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{f}}_2 \| \leq c \sqrt{n}$  holds with very high probability, so (114) and (116) indicate that we only need to prove that  $\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1) \geq c_0$ .

step 2. we next prove a the claimed lower bound on  $\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{u}_1^\top \mathbf{u}_1)$ . we will make use of the augmented infinite-width kernel

$$
[ \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {0} ] _ {i j} := k (\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}), \qquad \text {w h e r e} i, j \leq n _ {0}.
$$

notice that the eigen-decomposition of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_0$  takes the same form as the one of  $\mathbf{k}$  as given in lemma 6 (with the change that  $n$  should be replaced by  $n_0$ ). we denote by  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0 \in \mathbb{r}^{n_0 \times d_0}$  the groups of eigenvectors that correspond to  $\mathbf{u}$  in that lemma and have the same eigenvalue structure as  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}$ . namely, in the eigen-decomposition of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_0$ , we keep eigenvalues from the largest  $\ell_0$  groups in (109) and let columns of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0$  be the corresponding eigenvectors. we can express  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0$  and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}$  as  $\ell_0$  groups of eigenvectors.

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} = \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(1)}, \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(2)}, \dots , \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(\ell_ {0})} \right], \quad \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} = \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(1)}, \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(2)}, \dots , \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(\ell_ {0})} \right]. \tag {117}
$$

the remaining  $\ell +2 - \ell_0$  groups of eigenvectors are denoted by  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}^{(\ell_{0} + 1)},\ldots ,\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_{0}^{(\ell +2)}$ , and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(\ell_0 + 1)},\ldots ,\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(\ell +2)}$ . note that

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} = \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}, \qquad \text {w h e r e} \mathbf {p} _ {1} := \left( \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {i} _ {n} & \mathbf {0} \\ \mathbf {0} & \mathbf {0} \end{array} \right).
$$

define  $\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_0^\perp = \mathbf{i}_{n_0} - \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0^\top$ . we apply lemma 9 where we set  $\mathbf{u}_a = \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0$ ,  $\mathbf{u}_b = \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}$ ,  $\mathbf{p} = \widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_1$ , and  $\mathbf{p}_a^\perp = \widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_0^\perp$ . this yields

$$
\left[ \lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}\right) \right] ^ {1 / 2} = \sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}\right) \geq \sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}\right) \sigma_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}\right) - \sigma_ {\max } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}\right). \tag {118}
$$

now we invoke lemma 7 (a) for the pair  $(k,k^{\prime})$  where  $k\leq \ell_0$  and  $k^{\prime} > \ell_{0}$ . by the definition of  $\ell_0$ , the assumption of lemma 7 (a) is satisfied, so  $\| (\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_0^{(k')})^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(k)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \check{\sigma}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$ . thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {\max } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}\right) = \left\| \sum_ {k ^ {\prime} > \ell_ {0}} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sum_ {k ^ {\prime} > \ell_ {0}} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \sum_ {k ^ {\prime} > \ell_ {0}} \sum_ {k \leq \ell_ {0}} \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (\ell + 2) ^ {2} \cdot \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \\ \end{array}
$$

moreover, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sigma_ {\min } (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}}) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \stackrel {(i)} {\underset {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} {\min }} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} - \widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \\ \geq \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| - \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \stackrel {(i i)} {=} 1 - \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \boldsymbol {v} \right\| \\ \geq 1 - \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {0} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \geq 1 - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1), \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  and  $(ii)$  uses the fact that  $\| \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0\pmb {a}\| = \| \pmb {a}\|$  (where  $\pmb{a}$  is a vector) since  $\mathbf{u}_0$  has orthonormal columns.

finally, we claim that

$$
\sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}\right) \geq c, \tag {119}
$$

with very high probability where  $c > 0$  is a small constant. we defer the proof of this claim to step 4. once it is proved, from eqs. (118), we deduce that  $\lambda_{\mathrm{min}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1) \geq c'$ . from (114) and (116), we conclude that, with very high probability,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} ^ {2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 1} \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c, \\ \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} ^ {\top} \right] _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c, \\ \end{array}
$$

and thus we have proved (105) and (106).

step 3. we only prove (107) since (108) is similar. for simplicity, we denote  $z = \left\| (\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11})^{-1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}^{2})_{11}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11})^{-1} - \mathbf{i}_{n}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ . we know by theorem 3.2 that there exists a constant  $c' > 0$  such that  $\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}) \geq c'$  with very high probability. let  $c = c_1$  be the same constant as in eq. (105). for any  $\beta > 0$ , we also notice that the following event happens with very high probability

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime \prime}}} (z > c) > d ^ {- \beta}. \tag {120}
$$

(note that the left-hand side is a random variable depending on  $\pmb{x}_1, \dots, \pmb{x}_n$  and  $\pmb{w}_1, \dots, \pmb{w}_n$ .) indeed, for any  $\beta' > 0$ , by markov's inequality,

$$
\begin{array}{l} d ^ {\beta^ {\prime}} \mathbb {p} \left(\mathbb {p} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} (z > c) > d ^ {- \beta}\right) \leq d ^ {\beta + \beta^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbb {p} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} (z > c) \right] \\ = d ^ {\beta + \beta^ {\prime}} \mathbb {p} (z > c) \xrightarrow {d \to \infty} 0. \\ \end{array}
$$

thus, we proved that (120) happens with high probability. let  $\mathcal{a}$  be the event such that both  $\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}) \geq c'$  and (120) happen. by the assumption on  $\sigma'$ , namely assumption 3.2, on the event  $\lambda_{\min}(\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}_{11}) \geq c'$ , a deterministic (and naive) bound on  $\|\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}\|_{\max}$  is  $o(d^{2b})$ . so on  $\mathcal{a}$ , we get

$$
z \leq 1 + \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq c d ^ {2 b}.
$$

thus, on the event  $\mathcal{a}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime}}} [ z ] \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime}}} [ z; z \leq c ] + \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime}}} [ z; z > c ] \\ \leq c + c d ^ {2 b} \cdot \mathbb {p} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n + n ^ {\prime}}} (z > c). \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore,  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{x}_{n + 1},\dots,\boldsymbol{x}_{n + n'}}[z]\leq c'$  for some  $c^\prime >c$

step 4. we now prove the claim (119), namely  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{p}_1\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0)\geq c$  which was used in step 2. we use a similar strategy as in step 2. let  $\widetilde{\psi}_{\leq \ell}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_0\times d}$  contain the normalized spherical harmonics evaluated on  $x_{1},\ldots ,x_{n_{0}}$ . similar to (117), in the eigen-decomposition of  $\widetilde{\psi}_{\leq \ell}\lambda_{\leq \ell}^2\widetilde{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top +\gamma_{> \ell}\mathbf{i}_{n_0}$ , we keep eigenvalues from the largest  $\ell_0$  groups in (109) and let  $\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_s$  be those eigenvalues and columns of  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s$  be the corresponding eigenvectors. then we partition  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s,\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_s$  into  $\ell_0$  groups:

$$
\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s} = \big [ \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(2)}, \ldots , \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(\ell_ {0})} \big ], \qquad \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {s} = \mathrm {d i a g} \big (\widetilde {d} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {s} ^ {(2)}, \ldots , \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {s} ^ {(\ell_ {0})} \big).
$$

the remaining  $\ell + 2 - \ell_0$  groups of eigenvectors are denoted by  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_s^{(\ell_0 + 1)}, \ldots, \widetilde{\mathbf{v}}_s^{(\ell + 2)}$ . define  $\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_s^\perp = \mathbf{i}_{n_0} - \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s^\top$ . we apply lemma 9 again, in which we set  $\mathbf{u}_a = \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s$ ,  $\mathbf{u}_b = \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0$ ,  $\mathbf{p} = \mathbf{p}_1$ , and  $\mathbf{p}_a^\perp = \widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_s^\perp$ . this yields

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}) \geq \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s}) \sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {s} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}) - \sigma_ {\mathrm {m a x}} (\widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {s} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}).
$$

by lemma 8, we have  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{p}_1\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s)\geq c > 0$  with very high probability for certain constant  $c$ . because of the gap  $\lambda_{(\ell_0 + 1)}\leq \delta_0\lambda_{(\ell_0)}$ , we can apply lemma 7: for  $k^{\prime} > \ell_{0}$  and  $k\leq \ell_0$ , we have

$$
\big \| (\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \big \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 \| \pmb {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{| \lambda_ {k} - \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} | - \check {\sigma} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)} \leq \frac {2 \| \pmb {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{(1 - \delta_ {0}) \lambda_ {(\ell_ {0})} - \check {\sigma} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)} \leq \frac {3 (1 + \check {\sigma} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)) \| \pmb {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{\gamma_ {> \ell}}.
$$

further  $\| \pmb{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = \check{o}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$  by lemma 6. thus,

$$
\sigma_ {\max } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {p}} _ {s} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}\right) \leq \sum_ {k ^ {\prime} > \ell_ {0}} \sum_ {k \leq \ell_ {0}} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1), \tag {121}
$$

moreover,  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0)\geq 1 - \| \widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_s^\perp \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq 1 - \check{o}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$ . combining the lower bounds on  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\mathbf{p}_1\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s)$ ,  $\sigma_{\mathrm{min}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_s^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_0)$  and the upper bound (121), we obtain

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} _ {1} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {0}) \geq (1 - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot c ^ {\prime} - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)
$$

with very high probability. this proves the claim.

# b.3 proof of lemma 4

by homogeneity, we will assume, without loss of generality  $\| f\|_{l^2} = 1$

recall that, by lemma 1, we have  $k(\pmb{x}_i, \pmb{x}) = \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} \gamma_k q_k^{(d)}(\langle \pmb{x}_i, \pmb{x} \rangle)$  (where we replaced  $\pmb{x}_j$  by an independent copy  $\pmb{x}$  for notational convenience). we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} k _ {i j} ^ {(2)} = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ k (\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x}) k (\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}) \right] \stackrel {(i)} {=} \lim  _ {m \rightarrow \infty} \sum_ {k, m = 0} ^ {m} \gamma_ {k} \gamma_ {m} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle) q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle) \right] \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {=} \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\infty} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \pmb {x} _ {i}, \pmb {x} _ {j} \rangle) \\ \end{array}
$$

where in (i) we used  $\lim_{m\to \infty}\| \sum_{k > m}\gamma_kq_k^{(d)}(\langle \pmb {x}_i,\cdot \rangle)\|_{l^2} = 0$  and convergence in  $l^2$ -spaces (lemma 24), and in (ii) we used the identity (40). note that

$$
\gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {(2)} := \sum_ {k > \ell} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , \ell)} \leq \frac {1}{b (d , \ell + 1)} \sum_ {k > \ell} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \leq \frac {(1 + o _ {d} (1)) (\ell !) \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {2}}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}.
$$

using the identity (41), we have  $\mathbf{q}_k \coloneqq (\mathbf{q}_k^{(d)}(\langle \mathbf{x}_i, \mathbf{x}_j \rangle))_{i,j \leq n} = [b(d,k)]^{-1}\mathbf{\phi}_{= \ell}\mathbf{\phi}_{= \ell}^{\top}$ . thus, we can express  $\mathbf{k}^{(2)}$  as

$$
\mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} = \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {(2)} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(2)} \tag {122}
$$

where  $\pmb{\delta}^{(2)}\coloneqq \sum_{k > \ell}[b(d,k)]^{-1}\gamma_k^2 (\pmb {q}_k - \mathbf{i}_n)$  satisfies, by proposition e.1, with high probability,

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(2)} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {(2)} \sup  _ {k > \ell} \left\| \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} - \mathbf {i} _ {d} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \sqrt {\frac {n (\log n) ^ {c}}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}}.
$$

using the decomposition of  $\mathbf{k}^{(2)}$  in (122) and  $\| \mathbf{k}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c$  from lemma 2, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(2)} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {(2)}\right) \cdot \| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \\ \leq \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \frac {c}{n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

now we use the identity (113), in which we set  $\mathbf{a} = \lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \mathbf{k}$ ,  $\mathbf{a}_0 = \psi_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top + \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$ , and  $\mathbf{h} = \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^4 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top$ . it holds that  $\| \mathbf{a}^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$ ,  $\| \mathbf{a}_0^{-1} \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$ , and  $\| \mathbf{a} - \mathbf{a}_0 \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$  with high probability. so with high probability,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq c \cdot \left\| \left(\psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {4} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \left(\psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} c \cdot \left\| \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \big (\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {- 2} \big) ^ {- 2} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq c \cdot \left\| \psi_ {\leq \ell} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \lambda_ {\min } \left(\psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \psi_ {\leq \ell}\right) ^ {- 2} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} \frac {c}{n}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  follows from the identity (156) and  $(ii)$  follows from (82). this completes the proof of (74).

in order to prove (75), we will prove that the following holds w.h.p.

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathbb {s} ^ {n - 1}} \left| \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right| \leq \frac {c}{\sqrt {n}}.
$$

we use the cauchy-schwarz inequality on the left-hand side. since we assumed, without loss of generality,  $\| f\|_{l^2} = 1$ , we only need to show

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \right] \leq \frac {c}{n}
$$

for any unit vector  $\pmb{u}$ . this is immediate from (74) since

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sup  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| = 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \sup  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| = 1} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \right] \boldsymbol {u} \\ = \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the proof is now complete.

# b.4 proof of lemma 5

proof of lemma 5. step 1: proving (76)-(78). define, for  $k \leq n$ ,

$$
k ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}) = \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} \rangle) \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \rangle}{d}
$$

so we have  $\mathbf{k}_n = n^{-1}\sum_{k\leq n}\mathbf{k}^{(k)}$ . we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ (\delta i _ {1 2} ^ {g, h}) ^ {2} \right] = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k, k ^ {\prime} \leq n} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \left(\left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1})\right) \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot)\right) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2})\right) \boldsymbol {v} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k \leq n} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1})\right) \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot)\right) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}) \right] \boldsymbol {v} \\ = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k \leq n} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot)\right) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}) \right] \boldsymbol {v} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used independence of  $\mathbf{k}^{(k)}$  (conditional on  $(\pmb{x}_i)_{i\leq n}$ ) and  $\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{w}}[\mathbf{k}^{(k)}] = \mathbf{k}$ . note that

$$
\left. \right. \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}) \right] \boldsymbol {v} = \left(\boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z}) g (\boldsymbol {z}) \right]\right) ^ {2} \geq 0,
$$

and that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {z} _ {2}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}, \cdot) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {1}) g (\boldsymbol {z} _ {2}) \right] = \mathbf {h} _ {1}.
$$

this proves the bound on  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{z}_1,\boldsymbol{z}_2}[(\delta i_{12}^{g,\boldsymbol{h}})^2]$  in (76). a similar bound, namely (77), holds for  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{w},\boldsymbol{z}_1,\boldsymbol{z}_2}[(\delta i_{231}^{\boldsymbol{h}})^2]$ , in which we replace  $g$  with  $\widetilde{h}$ .

next, we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \delta i _ {2 3 2} ^ {\boldsymbol {h}} \right] = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k, k ^ {\prime} \leq n} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})\right) \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot)\right) \right] \boldsymbol {v} \\ = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k \leq n} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})\right) \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot)\right) \right] \boldsymbol {v} \\ = \frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \sum_ {k \leq n} \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot) - \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) \mathbf {k} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot) \right] \boldsymbol {v}. \\ \end{array}
$$

note that  $\mathbf{k}(\cdot ,\pmb {x})\mathbf{k}(\pmb {x},\cdot)$  is p.s.d., and that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) \mathbf {k} ^ {(k)} (\boldsymbol {x}, \cdot) \right] = \mathbf {h} _ {3}.
$$

this proves the bound on  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{w}}\big[\delta i_{232}^{\boldsymbol{h}}\big]$  in (78).

step 2: proving the bounds on  $\mathbf{h}_1, \mathbf{h}_2, \mathbf{h}_3$ . we define the function  $q(\boldsymbol{w}) \in \mathbb{r}^d$  as follows.

$$
\boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) = \frac {1}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {z}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) g (\boldsymbol {z}) \boldsymbol {z} \right].
$$

we observe that, for any unit vector  $\pmb{u} \in \mathbb{r}^d$ ,

$$
\left| \langle \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}), \boldsymbol {u} \rangle \right| \leq \frac {1}{d} \left\{\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \left(\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {z}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle)\right) ^ {4} \right] \right\} ^ {1 / 4} \cdot \left\{\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ g (\boldsymbol {z}) ^ {2} \right] \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \cdot \left\{\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \langle \boldsymbol {u}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle^ {4} \right] \right\} ^ {1 / 4} \leq \frac {c}{d} \| g \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2},
$$

where we used hölder's inequality and

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[\left(\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {z}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle)\right) ^ {4} \right] = \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[\left(\sigma^ {\prime} \left(z _ {1}\right)\right) ^ {4} \right] = \mathbb {e} _ {g \sim \mathcal {n} (0, 1)} \left[\left(\sigma^ {\prime} (g)\right) ^ {4} \right], \tag {123}
$$

$$
\lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \langle \boldsymbol {u}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle^ {4} \right] = \lim  _ {d \rightarrow \infty} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ z _ {1} ^ {4} \right] = \mathbb {e} _ {g \sim \mathcal {n} (0, 1)} \left[ g ^ {4} \right]. \tag {124}
$$

since  $\| g\|_{l^2}$  is independent of  $\pmb{w}$ , it follows that

$$
\sup  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| = 1} \left\| \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \right\| = \sup  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| = \| \boldsymbol {u} \| = 1} \left| \langle \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}), \boldsymbol {u} \rangle \right| \leq \frac {c}{d} \| g \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2}.
$$

using this function, we express  $\mathbf{h}_1$  as

$$
h _ {1} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}) = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \langle \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} \rangle \langle \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \rangle \right].
$$

let  $\pmb{\alpha} = (\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})^{\top}$  be any vector, then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {\alpha} ^ {\top} \mathbf {h} _ {1} \boldsymbol {\alpha} = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) ^ {\top} \left(\sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \left\| \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \right\| ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \right] \\ \leq \frac {c \| g \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2}}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {2}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} ^ {\top}\right) \right] \\ = c \| g \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} \sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} , \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle}{d} \right] \\ = c \| g \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} \cdot \boldsymbol {\alpha} ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} \boldsymbol {\alpha} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\| \mathbf{a}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{a})$  for a p.s.d. matrix  $\mathbf{a}$ . this shows  $\mathbf{h}_1\spreceq cd^{-1}\| g\|_{l^2}^2\mathbf{k}$ . the proof for  $\mathbf{h}_2$  is similar.

we also define  $\pmb{q}(\pmb{w}) \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times n}$ :

$$
\boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \big [ \left(\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle)\right) ^ {2} \frac {\boldsymbol {z} \boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top}}{d} \big ].
$$

by definition,  $q(\boldsymbol{w})$  is p.s.d. for all  $\boldsymbol{w}$ . for any  $\boldsymbol{w}$  and unit vector  $\boldsymbol{u}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \boldsymbol {u} = \frac {1}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \left(\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle)\right) ^ {2} \langle \boldsymbol {u}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle^ {2} \right] \\ \leq \frac {1}{d} \left\{\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {z}} \left[ \left(\sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {w}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle)\right) ^ {4} \right] \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \cdot \left\{\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \langle \boldsymbol {u}, \boldsymbol {z} \rangle^ {4} \right] \right\} ^ {1 / 2} \leq \frac {c}{d} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the cauchy-schwarz inequality and (123)-(124). this implies

$$
\sup  _ {\| \boldsymbol {w} \| = 1} \left\| \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {c}{d}.
$$

for any vector  $\pmb{\alpha} = (\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n})^{\top}$ , we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {\alpha} ^ {\top} \mathbf {h} _ {3} \boldsymbol {\alpha} = \frac {1}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right] \\ = \frac {1}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w})\right) \right] \\ \leq \frac {1}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \left\| \boldsymbol {q} (\boldsymbol {w}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left(\sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} ^ {\top}\right) \right] \\ \leq \frac {c}{d} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ \sum_ {i, j \leq n} \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {j} \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \sigma^ {\prime} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}, \boldsymbol {w} \rangle) \frac {\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i} , \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle}{d} \right] \\ = \frac {c}{d} \pmb {\alpha} ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} \pmb {\alpha}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{a}_1\mathbf{a}_2)\leq \| \mathbf{a}_1\|_{\mathrm{op}}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{a}_2)$  for p.s.d. matrices  $\mathbf{a}_1,\mathbf{a}_2$  . this proves  $\mathbf{h}_3\succeq cd^{-1}\mathbf{k}$

step 3: proving the "as a consequence" part. now we derive bounds on  $\delta i_{12}^{g,h}$  and  $\delta i_{23}^h$ . by assumption,  $\| g\|_{l^2}$  is bounded by a constant.

also, by assumption  $\| \pmb {h}\| ^2 /n\leq c$  with high probability. therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left[ (\delta i _ {1 2} ^ {g, \boldsymbol {h}}) ^ {2} \right] \leq \frac {1}{n d} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} \leq \frac {1}{n d} \boldsymbol {h} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} \\ \leq \frac {1}{c n d} \| \boldsymbol {h} \| ^ {2} \leq \frac {c n}{n d}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similar bounds hold for  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{w}}\big[(\delta i_{231}^{\boldsymbol{h}})^{2}\big]$  and  $\mathbb{e}_{\boldsymbol{w}}\big[\delta i_{232}^{\boldsymbol{h}}\big]$ . note that  $\delta i_{232}^{\boldsymbol{h}}$  is always nonnegative. by markov's inequality, we have w.h.p.,

$$
| \delta i _ {1 2} ^ {g, h} | \leq \sqrt {\frac {c n \log n}{n d}}, \quad | \delta i _ {2 3 1} ^ {h} | \leq \sqrt {\frac {c n \log n}{n d}}, \quad | \delta i _ {2 3 2} ^ {h} | \leq \frac {c n \log n}{n d}.
$$

combining the last two bounds then leads to the bound on  $|\delta i_{23}^h|$ .

# b.5 proof of theorem 8.2

by homogeneity we can and will assume, without loss of generality,  $\| f\|_{l^2} = \sigma_\varepsilon = 1$ . it is convenient to introduce the following matrix  $\mathbf{k}^{(p,2)}\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times n}$

$$
\mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} = \left(\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ k ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x}\right) k ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j}\right) \right]\right) _ {i, j \leq n}. \tag {125}
$$

we further define  $\overline{\mathbf{k}}^p \coloneqq \mathbf{k}^p + \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$  or, equivalently,

$$
\overline {{k}} _ {i j} ^ {p} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\ell} \gamma_ {k} q _ {k} ^ {(d)} \left(\left\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \right\rangle\right) + \gamma_ {> \ell} \delta_ {i j}. \tag {126}
$$

we will use the following lemma.

lemma 11. suppose that  $c_1 > 0$  is a constant and that  $\pmb{h}_1, \pmb{h}_2 \in \mathbb{r}^n$  are random vectors that satisfy  $\max \{\| \pmb{h}_1 \|, \| \pmb{h}_2 \| \} \leq c_1 \sqrt{n}$  with high probability. then, there exists a constant  $c' > 0$  such

that the following bounds hold with high probability.

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} - \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} \right| \leq \sqrt {\frac {c ^ {\prime} (\log n) ^ {c ^ {\prime}} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}}, \tag {127}
$$

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] - \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right| \leq \sqrt {\frac {c ^ {\prime} (\log n) ^ {c ^ {\prime}} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} \tag {128}
$$

we first show that theorem 8.2 follows from this lemma. after that, we will prove lemma 11.

proof of theorem 8.2. first we observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {\text {b i a s}} - e _ {\text {b i a s}} ^ {p} = - 2 \left(\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] - \boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right]\right) \\ + \left(\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f} - \boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

in lemma 11 eqs. (127) and (128), we set  $\pmb{h}_1 = \pmb{h}_2 = \pmb{f}$ , which yields  $|e_{\mathrm{bias}} - e_{\mathrm{bias}}^p| \leq \eta'$ . for the variance term, we apply lemma 11 eq. (127) with  $\pmb{h}_1 = \pmb{h}_2 = \pmb{\varepsilon}$ , which yields  $|e_{\mathrm{var}} - e_{\mathrm{var}}^p| \leq \eta'$ .

for the cross term, we observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {\text {c r o s s}} - e _ {\text {c r o s s}} ^ {p} = \left(\varepsilon^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] - \varepsilon^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right]\right) \\ - \left(\varepsilon^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f} - \varepsilon^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

we apply lemma 11 with  $\pmb{h}_1 = \pmb{\varepsilon}$  and  $\pmb{h}_2 = \pmb{f}$ . this leads to  $|e_{\mathrm{cross}} - e_{\mathrm{cross}}^p| \leq \eta'$ , which completes the proof.

proof of lemma 11. define differences

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta i _ {1} ^ {\prime} = \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)}\right) \left(\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} \\ \delta i _ {2} ^ {\prime} = \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} - \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2}, \\ \delta i _ {3} ^ {\prime} = \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right], \\ \delta i _ {4} ^ {\prime} = \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} \big [ (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} - (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \big ] \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \big [ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \big ]. \\ \end{array}
$$

we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} - \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} = \delta i _ {1} ^ {\prime} + \delta i _ {2} ^ {\prime}, \\ \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) ] - \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} [ \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) ] = \delta i _ {3} ^ {\prime} + \delta i _ {4} ^ {\prime}. \\ \end{array}
$$

we observe that

$$
\overline {{k}} _ {i j} ^ {p} = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\ell} \gamma_ {k} q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle) + \gamma_ {> \ell} \delta_ {i j},
$$

$$
k _ {i j} ^ {(p, 2)} = \sum_ {k, m = 0} ^ {\ell} \gamma_ {k} \gamma_ {m} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \big [ q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} \rangle) q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle) \big ] = \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {\ell} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} q _ {k} ^ {(d)} (\langle \boldsymbol {x} _ {i}, \boldsymbol {x} _ {j} \rangle).
$$

it follows that

$$
\mathbf {k} - \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p} = \sum_ {k > \ell} \gamma_ {k} \left(\mathbf {q} _ {k} - \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right), \quad \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} = \sum_ {k > \ell} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} \mathbf {q} _ {k}, \tag {129}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})) (\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})) ^ {\top} \right] = \sum_ {k > \ell} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} q _ {k}. \tag {130}
$$

therefore, with high probability,

$$
