\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {k} - \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \sum_ {k > \ell} \gamma_ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {q} _ {k} - \mathbf {i} _ {n}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \gamma_ {> \ell} \sup  _ {k > \ell} \left\| \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \overset {(i)} {\leq} \sqrt {\frac {c (\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}}, (131) \\ \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \sum_ {k > \ell} \frac {\gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sum_ {k > \ell} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \cdot \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \sup  _ {k > \ell} \| \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} (132) \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {2} \cdot \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \cdot (1 + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \leq \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}, (133) \\ \end{array}
$$

where in  $(i)$ ,  $(ii)$  we used proposition e.1. also, note that  $\overline{\mathbf{k}}^p\succeq \gamma_{> \ell}\mathbf{i}_n$ , so we must have  $\| (\lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \overline{\mathbf{k}}^p)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \gamma_{> \ell}^{-1}$ . thus, w.h.p.,

$$
\left| \delta i _ {1} ^ {\prime} \right| \leq \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} \| \cdot \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} \| \cdot \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} - \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {c n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}.
$$

in the identity (113), we set  $\mathbf{a}_0 = \lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \overline{\mathbf{k}}^p$ ,  $\mathbf{a} = \lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \mathbf{k}$ , and  $\mathbf{h} = \mathbf{k}^{(2)}$ . it holds that w.h.p.,  $\| \mathbf{a}_0^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}, \| \mathbf{a}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c$ ,  $\| \mathbf{a} - \mathbf{a}_0\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \sqrt{c(\log n)^c n / d^{\ell + 1}}$ . thus, w.h.p.,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \delta i _ {2} ^ {\prime} \right| \leq c \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} \| \cdot \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {2} \| \cdot \sqrt {\frac {c (\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \cdot \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(2)} \big (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} \big) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c n \cdot \sqrt {\frac {(\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \cdot \frac {c}{n} \\ \leq \sqrt {\frac {c (\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used lemma 4 eq. (74). combining the upper bounds on  $|\delta i_1'|$  and  $|\delta i_2'|$ , we arrive at the first inequality in the lemma.

next, we bound  $|\delta i_3'|$ . with high probability,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left. \right.\left| \delta i _ {3} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \right.\left( \right.\boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})\right) ^ {2} \left. \right] \cdot \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (f (\boldsymbol {x})) ^ {2} \right]\left. \right. \\ \leq \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ (\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})) (\mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x})) ^ {\top} \right] (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {1} \cdot \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} \sum_ {k > \ell} \frac {c \gamma_ {k} ^ {2}}{b (d , k)} \cdot \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} \\ \leq \frac {c}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \cdot \sum_ {k > \ell} \gamma_ {k} ^ {2} \cdot \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} \| ^ {2} \cdot \| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \| \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i i i)} {\leq} \frac {c n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \cdot \gamma_ {> \ell} ^ {2} \cdot \sup  _ {k > \ell} \| q _ {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \stackrel {(i v)} {\leq} \frac {c n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $(i)$  follows from the cauchy-schwarz inequality,  $(ii)$  follows from (130),  $(iii)$  is because  $\| \pmb {h}\| \leq c\sqrt{n}$  w.h.p. by assumption and  $\| (\lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \overline{\mathbf{k}}^p)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \gamma_{>l}^{-1}$ , and  $(iv)$  follows from proposition e.1.

finally,

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \delta i _ {4} ^ {\prime} | \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {h} _ {1} ^ {\top} (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \right\| \cdot \| \overline {{\mathbf {k}}} ^ {p} - \mathbf {k} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| (\lambda \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \big [ \mathbf {k} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \big ] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} c \sqrt {n} \cdot \sqrt {\frac {c (\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \cdot \frac {c}{\sqrt {n}} \stackrel {(i i)} {\leq} \sqrt {\frac {c (\log n) ^ {c} n}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in (i) we used  $\| (\lambda \mathbf{i}_n + \overline{\mathbf{k}}^p)^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \gamma_{> \ell}^{-1}$ , eq. (131), and lemma 4 eq. (75). combining the upper bounds on  $|\delta i_3^{\prime}|$  and  $|\delta i_4^{\prime}|$ , we arrive at the second inequality of this lemma.

# c generalization error: improved analysis for  $\ell = 1$

we will show that for the case  $\ell = 1$ , we can relax the condition

$$
c _ {0} (\log d) ^ {c _ {0}} d \leq n \leq \frac {d ^ {2}}{c _ {0} (\log d) ^ {c _ {0}}} \qquad \mathrm {t o} \qquad c _ {0} d \leq n \leq \frac {d ^ {2}}{c _ {0} (\log d) ^ {c _ {0}}}.
$$

the proof of the generalization error under this relaxed condition follows mostly the proof in section b.2, with modifications we show in this subsection.

throughout, we suppose that  $\ell = 1$  and correspondingly  $d = d + 1$ . the matrix  $\psi_{\leq \ell} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$  is given by

$$
\boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} = \left(\mathbf {1} _ {n}, \boldsymbol {x}\right).
$$

an important difference under the relaxed condition is that  $n^{-1}\psi_{\leq \ell}^{\top}\psi_{\leq \ell}$  does not necessarily converge to  $\mathbf{i}_d$  (cf. eq. 51). (in fact, if  $n,d$  satisfy  $n / d\to \kappa$ , the spectra of  $n^{-1}\pmb {x}^{\top}\pmb {x}$  is characterized by the marchenko-pastur distribution.) nevertheless, if  $n\geq cd$  where  $c$  is sufficiently large, we have a good control of  $n^{-1}\psi_{\leq \ell}^{\top}\psi_{\leq \ell}$ .

lemma 12. for any constant  $\delta \in (0,0.1)$ , there exists a certain  $c_{\delta} > 1$  such that the following holds. if  $n \geq c_{\delta}d$ , then with very high probability,

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{n} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} - \mathbf {i} _ {d} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \delta . \tag {134}
$$

fix the constant  $\delta = 0.01$ . let  $c_{\delta} > 1$  be the constant in lemma 12. first, we establish some useful results under the condition

$$
c _ {\delta} d \leq n \leq \frac {d ^ {2}}{c (\log d) ^ {c}}, \quad n \leq \frac {n d}{(\log (n d)) ^ {c}}, \tag {135}
$$

for a sufficiently large  $c > 0$  such that the following inequalities hold by lemma 2, 12 and theorem 3.2: in the decomposition  $\mathbf{k} = \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n + \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell} \boldsymbol{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2 \boldsymbol{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top + \boldsymbol{\delta}$ , we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1), \quad \left\| n ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1), \quad \left\| \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \mathbf {k}\right) \mathbf {k} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \tag {136}
$$

we strengthen lemma 6 for the case  $\ell = 1$ .

lemma 13 (kernel eigenvalue structure: case  $\ell = 1$ ). assume that eq. (135) holds and set  $\ell = 1$  and  $d = d + 1$ . then the eigen-decomposition of  $\mathbf{k} - \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$  and  $\mathbf{k}_n - \gamma_{> \ell} \mathbf{i}_n$  can be written in the following form:

$$
\mathbf {k} - \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n} = \mathbf {u} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {u} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\delta} ^ {(\text {r e s})}, \tag {137}
$$

$$
\mathbf {k} _ {n} - \gamma_ {> \ell} \mathbf {i} _ {n} = \mathbf {u} _ {n} \mathbf {d} _ {n} \mathbf {u} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {n} ^ {(\text {r e s})}, \tag {138}
$$

where  $\mathbf{d},\mathbf{d}_n\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  are diagonal matrices that contain  $d$  eigenvalues of  $\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}_n$  respectively, columns of  $\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}_n\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times d}$  are the corresponding eigenvectors and  $\pmb{\delta}^{\mathrm{(res)}},\pmb{\delta}_{n}^{\mathrm{(res)}}$  correspond to the other eigenvectors (in particular  $\pmb{\delta}^{\mathrm{(res)}}\mathbf{u} = \pmb{\delta}_{n}^{\mathrm{(res)}}\mathbf{u}_{n} = \mathbf{0}$ ).

further defining

$$
\mathbf {d} ^ {*} = \mathrm {d i a g} \big (\gamma_ {0} n, \underbrace {\frac {\gamma_ {1} n}{d} , \ldots , \frac {\gamma_ {1} n}{d}} _ {d} \big).
$$

the eigenvalues have the following structure:

$$
(1 - 2 \delta - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*} - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \leq \mathbf {d} \leq (1 + 2 \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*} + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1), \tag {139}
$$

$$
(1 - 2 \delta - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*} - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \leq \mathbf {d} _ {n} \leq (1 + 2 \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*} + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \tag {140}
$$

here,  $\leq$  denotes entrywise comparisons ( $\mathbf{a} \leq \mathbf{a}'$  if and only if  $a_{ij} \leq a_{ij}'$  for all  $i, j$ ). moreover, the remaining components satisfy  $\| \pmb{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})} \|_{\mathrm{op}} = \check{o}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$  and  $\| \pmb{\delta}_n^{(\mathrm{res})} \|_{\mathrm{op}} = \check{o}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$ .

proof of lemma 13. in the proof of lemma 6, instead of claiming (93), we will show that the following modified claim holds.

$$
(1 - 2 \delta - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*} \leq \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q}) \leq (1 + 2 \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \mathbf {d} ^ {*}.
$$

to prove this claim, we note that lemma 12 implies that  $n^{-1/2} \|\psi_{\leq \ell}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \sqrt{1 + \delta} \leq 1 + \delta$  and  $n^{-1/2} \|\psi_{\leq \ell}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \geq \sqrt{1 - \delta} \geq 1 - \delta$  with very high probability. following the notations in the proof of lemma 6, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q}\right) = \max  _ {\mathcal {v}: \dim (\mathcal {v}) = k} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {0} \neq \boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {v}} \frac {\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \boldsymbol {u}}{\| \boldsymbol {u} \| ^ {2}} \\ \geq \left(1 - 2 \delta - \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \min  _ {\mathbf {0} \neq \mathbf {u} \in \mathcal {v} _ {1} ^ {\prime}} \frac {\mathbf {u} ^ {\top} \mathbf {q} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \mathbf {u}}{\| \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \mathbf {u} \| ^ {2} / n} \\ \geq \left(1 - 2 \delta - \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \min  _ {\boldsymbol {0} \neq \boldsymbol {x} \in \mathcal {v} _ {1}} \frac {\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {x}}{\| \boldsymbol {x} \| ^ {2} / n} \\ \geq n (1 - 2 \delta - \breve {\partial} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \lambda_ {k} \left(\lambda_ {\leq \ell} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q}\right) = \min  _ {\mathcal {v}: \dim (\mathcal {v}) = d - k + 1} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {v}} \frac {\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \psi_ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \psi_ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \boldsymbol {u}}{\| \boldsymbol {u} \| ^ {2}} \\ \leq \left(1 + 2 \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {v} _ {2} ^ {\prime}} \frac {\boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \boldsymbol {u}}{\| \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {q} \boldsymbol {u} \| ^ {2} / n} \\ \leq \left(1 + 2 \delta + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)\right) \max  _ {\boldsymbol {x} \in \mathcal {v} _ {2}} \frac {\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {x}}{\| \boldsymbol {x} \| ^ {2} / n} \\ \leq n (1 + 2 \delta + \breve {\partial} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \lambda_ {k} (\boldsymbol {\lambda} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

we omit the rest of proof, as it follows closely the proof of lemma 6.

we prove a slight modification of lemma 7 for the case  $\ell = 1$ .

lemma 14 (kernel eigenvector structure: case  $\ell = 1$ ). assume that eq. (135) holds and set  $\ell = 1$  and  $d = d + 1$ . let  $k \neq k' \in \{0, 1, \ldots, \ell + 1\}$ . denote  $\lambda_k = \gamma_{> \ell} + \gamma_k(k!) n / (d^k)$  for  $k = 0, \ldots, \ell$  and  $\lambda_{\ell + 1} = \gamma_{> \ell}$ .

(a) suppose that  $\min \{\lambda_k / \lambda_{k'},\lambda_{k'} / \lambda_k\} \leq 1 / 4$  . then,

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1).
$$

(b) recall  $\pmb{\delta}^{(\mathrm{res})}$  defined in (138). if  $|\lambda_k - \lambda_{k'}| \geq 4.1\delta$ $\max \{\lambda_k, \lambda_{k'}\}$ , then

$$
\big \| (\mathbf {v} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k)} \big \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {2 \| \pmb {\delta} ^ {(\mathrm {r e s})} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{| \lambda_ {k} - \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} | - 4 \delta \max \{\lambda_ {k} , \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \} - \check {\sigma} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1)}.
$$

proof of lemma 14. instead of eqs. (96)-(97), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \min _ {i, j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right| \geq \left| \gamma_ {> \ell} + (1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) (\lambda_ {k} - \gamma_ {> \ell}) - \gamma_ {> \ell} - (1 + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) (\lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} - \gamma_ {> \ell}) \right| \\ - 2 \delta | \lambda_ {k} - \gamma_ {> \ell} | - 2 \delta | \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} - \gamma_ {> \ell} | - \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1) \\ \geq \left| (1 + \breve {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \lambda_ {k} - (1 + \breve {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \right| - 4 \delta \max \{\lambda_ {k}, \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \} - \breve {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1). \\ \end{array}
$$

by the assumptions on  $\lambda_{k}$  and  $\lambda_{k'}$ , with very high probability,

$$
\min  _ {i, j} \left| \left(\mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) _ {i i} - \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {j j} \right| \geq (1 / 2 - 4 \delta) \max  \{\lambda_ {k}, \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \}.
$$

also, instead of eqs. (98)-(99), we have

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {k} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \left[ \mathbf {d} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})} \right] ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (\sqrt {1 + 2 \delta} + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}}}.
$$

similarly,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} (\mathbf {d} ^ {(k)}) ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq (1 + \sqrt {1 + 2 \delta} + \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1)) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k}}.
$$

these inequalities lead to

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {v} _ {0} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}\right) ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\check {o} _ {d , \mathbb {p}} (1) \cdot \sqrt {\lambda_ {k} \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}}}}{(1 - 1 / 2 - 4 \delta) \max \{\lambda_ {k} , \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \}} \leq \check {o} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1),
$$

which results in the same conclusion as in lemma 7 (a). to show the modified inequality in (b), we only need to notice that

$$
\min _ {i, j} \left| (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k ^ {\prime})}) _ {i i} - (\mathbf {d} _ {s} ^ {(k)}) _ {j j} \right| \leq | \lambda_ {k} - \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} | - 4 \delta \max \{\lambda_ {k}, \lambda_ {k ^ {\prime}} \} - \check {\sigma} _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (1).
$$

the proof is complete.

recall that we denote the projection matrix  $\mathbf{p} = [e_1, \ldots, e_m][e_1, \ldots, e_m]^\top$ , and that the top- $d$  eigenvectors of  $\psi_{\leq \ell}^\top \lambda_{\leq \ell}^2 \psi_{\leq \ell}$  form  $\mathbf{u}_s$ . the next lemma adapts lemma 8 to the present case.

lemma 15. suppose that  $c'n \leq m \leq c'n$ , where  $c', c' \in (0,1)$  are constants. also suppose that the condition (135) is satisfied. then there exist  $c_3, c > 0$  such that if  $m > (c_3 + 1)d$ , then with very high probability,

$$
\sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {s}\right) \geq c.
$$

proof of lemma 15. following the proof of lemma 8, we have

$$
\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} \mathbf {u} _ {\psi}) \geq \frac {\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \mathbf {v} _ {\psi})}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {m a x}} (\mathbf {d} _ {\psi})} = \frac {\sigma_ {\mathrm {m i n}} (\mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell})}{\sigma_ {\mathrm {m a x}} (\mathbf {d} _ {\psi})}.
$$

by (136), we have  $n^{-1/2}\sigma_{\max}(\mathbf{d}_{\psi}) \leq 1 + \delta + \check{\sigma}_{d,\mathbb{p}}(1)$ . let  $\pmb{x}' \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times d}$  be the matrix formed by the top- $m$  rows of  $\pmb{x}$ . note that

$$
\sigma_ {\min } \left(\mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell}\right) = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| = 1} \left\| \mathbf {p} \boldsymbol {\psi} _ {\leq \ell} \boldsymbol {u} \right\| = \min  _ {\| \boldsymbol {u} \| = 1} \left\| \left[ \mathbf {1} _ {m}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime} \right] \boldsymbol {u} \right\| = \sigma_ {\min } \left(\left[ \mathbf {1} _ {m}, \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime} \right]\right)
$$

where the last equality holds since  $m \geq d + 1 = d$ . it suffices to show  $\sigma_{\min}\bigl ([\mathbf{1}_m,\mathbf{x}']\bigr) \geq c\sqrt{m} >0$  for certain constant  $c$  with very high probability.

let  $\mathbf{g} \coloneqq [\mathbf{1}_m, \mathbf{x}')^\top [\mathbf{1}_m, \mathbf{x}] / m$ , and note that

$$
\mathbf {g} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} 1 & \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \\ \boldsymbol {v} & \mathbf {g} _ {0} \end{array} \right], \quad \boldsymbol {v} := \frac {1}{m} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime}) ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {m}, \quad \mathbf {g} _ {0} := \frac {1}{m} (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\prime}.
$$

by concentration of the norm of subgaussian vectors, we have  $\| \pmb{v} \|_2 \leq 2\sqrt{d / m}$  with very high probability. by concentration of the eigenvalues of empirical covariance matrices [ver18], we have  $\lambda_0 := \lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{g}_0) \geq 1 - 3\sqrt{d / m}$  with very high probability. further, by the cauchy-schwarz inequality,

$$
\lambda_ {\min } (\mathbf {g}) \geq \lambda_ {\min } \left(\left[ \begin{array}{c c} 1 & \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} \\ \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} & \lambda_ {0} \end{array} \right]\right) = \frac {1}{2} \left[ 1 + \lambda_ {0} - \sqrt {(1 - \lambda_ {0}) ^ {2} + 4 \| \boldsymbol {v} \| _ {2} ^ {2}} \right].
$$

it follows from the above bounds that  $\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{g}) \geq 1/10$  with very high probability, provided we choose  $c_3$  a large enough constant. since  $\sigma_{\min}\big([1_m, x']\big) = \sqrt{m \lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{g})}$ , this implies the desired lower bound.

consider the condition  $c_0d\leq n\leq d^2 /(c(\log d)^c)$ . we choose  $n^\prime$  to be the smallest integer such that  $n^\prime \geq n + (c_0)^{-1}c_\delta n$ . as defined in (103), the augmented kernel matrix  $\widetilde{\mathbf{k}}$  has size  $n_0\times n_0$ , where  $n_0$  is guaranteed to satisfy  $n_0\geq c_\delta d$ .

by jensen's inequality,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {(2)} \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}},
$$

$$
\left\| \mathbf {k} _ {n} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \mathbf {k} _ {n} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) f (\boldsymbol {x}) \right] \right\| \leq \frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n _ {0}}} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\|
$$

where  $\widetilde{\pmb{f}}$  is defined in (104).

lemma 16. there exist constant  $c, c_2 > 0$  such that the following holds. if  $c_0 d \leq n \leq d^2 / (c (\log d)^c)$ , then with high probability,

$$
\frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} \| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {c _ {2}}{n}, \tag {141}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{n ^ {\prime}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x} _ {n + 1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {2 n}} \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \leq \frac {c _ {2}}{\sqrt {n}}. \tag {142}
$$

consequently, we obtain (67) and (68) in lemma 3 under the relaxed condition  $c_0 d \leq n \leq d^2 / (c(\log d)^c)$ .

we note that the remaining proof of lemma 3 is the same as before. once lemma 3 is proved, we will obtain theorem 3.3 under the relaxed condition for  $\ell = 1$ .

proof of lemma 16. following the proof of lemma 16, we have with very high probability,

$$
\left\| (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} ^ {2}) _ {1 1} (\widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c + c \cdot \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} \big (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \big) ^ {- 2} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}
$$

$$
\left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 1} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\mathbf {k}} _ {1 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \right\| \leq c \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \| + c \cdot \left\| \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {2} \|.
$$

as in the proof of lemma 16, it suffices to show

$$
\lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) \geq c \tag {143}
$$

for certain constant  $c > 0$ . in the case  $n > (c_3 + 1)d$ , the assumptions in lemma 13, 14, 15 are satisfied. following the proof of lemma 16, we have

$$
\lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1}\right) \geq c. \tag {144}
$$

below we consider the case  $n \leq (c_3 + 1)d$ . one difficulty is that we cannot apply lemma 15; moreover, if  $n < d$ , then the matrix  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1^\top \widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_1$  is not full rank, so we do not expect (144) to hold.

in order to resolve this issue, we first notice that if  $\gamma_0n_0\leq 2\gamma_1n_0 / d$ , then (143) holds with very high probability. in fact,

$$
\lambda_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \big (\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \big) \geq \lambda_ {\mathrm {m i n}} \big (\gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \big) \geq \frac {\gamma_ {> \ell}}{\max \{\gamma_ {0} n _ {0} , \gamma_ {1} n _ {0} / d \}} \geq \frac {\gamma_ {> \ell}}{2 \gamma_ {1} n _ {0} / d} > c.
$$

from now on, we assume  $\gamma_0n_0 > 2\gamma_1n_0 / d$ . conforming to our notations in the previous proof, we denote the top eigenvector (which corresponds to the eigenvalue closest to  $\gamma_0n_0$ ) of  $\mathbf{k}_n$ ,  $\mathbf{k}$ ,  $\widetilde{\psi}_{\leq \ell}\widetilde{\lambda}_{\leq \ell}^2\widetilde{\psi}_{\leq \ell}^\top$ , respectively, by  $\widetilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)},\widetilde{\pmb{v}}_{0}^{(1)},\widetilde{\pmb{v}}_{s}^{(1)}$ . also, denote  $\widetilde{\mathbf{u}} = [\widetilde{\pmb{v}}^{(1)},\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(2)}]$  where  $\widetilde{\mathbf{v}}^{(2)}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_0\times d}$ .

first, we make a claim about  $\pmb{v}_s^{(1)}$ . there exists a constant  $c_3 > 0$  such that if  $\gamma_0 n_0 \geq c_3$ , then for certain constant  $c_0' > 0$ , with very high probability,

$$
\left\| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \right\| \geq c _ {0} ^ {\prime}. \tag {145}
$$

to prove this claim, we express  $\widetilde{\pmb{v}}_s^{(1)}$  as

$$
\widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} = \operatorname {a r g m a x} _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left[ \boldsymbol {v} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {\leq \ell} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\lambda}} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\psi}} _ {\leq \ell} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {v} \right] = \operatorname {a r g m a x} _ {\| \boldsymbol {v} \| = 1} \left[ \gamma_ {0} \langle \boldsymbol {v}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} \rangle^ {2} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} \boldsymbol {v} \| ^ {2} \right]
$$

where each row of  $\widetilde{\pmb{x}}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_0\times d}$  is a uniform vector in  $\mathbb{s}^{d - 1}(\sqrt{d})$ . evaluating the maximization problem at  $\pmb {v} = \widetilde{\pmb{v}}_s^{(1)}$  and  $\pmb {v} = \mathbf{1}_{n_0} / \sqrt{n_0}$ , we obtain

$$
\gamma_ {0} \langle \widetilde {\pmb {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} \rangle^ {2} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \| \widetilde {\pmb {x}} \widetilde {\pmb {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \| ^ {2} \geq \gamma_ {0} n _ {0} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d n _ {0}} \| \widetilde {\pmb {x}} \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} \| ^ {2}.
$$

since  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{x}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 2(\sqrt{n_0} +\sqrt{d})\leq c'\sqrt{n_0}$  with very high probability, we have

$$
\gamma_ {0} \langle \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} \rangle^ {2} \geq \gamma_ {0} n _ {0} - c _ {4} \frac {n _ {0}}{d} \geq \gamma_ {0} n _ {0} - c _ {4} ^ {\prime}
$$

where  $c_4, c_4' > 0$  are some constants. thus, we obtain

$$
\langle \widetilde {\pmb {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} / \sqrt {n _ {0}} \rangle^ {2} \geq 1 - \frac {c _ {4} ^ {\prime}}{\gamma_ {0} n _ {0}}.
$$

we observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \langle \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} / \sqrt {n _ {0}} \rangle \leq \langle \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} / \sqrt {n _ {0}} \rangle + \langle \mathbf {p} ^ {\perp} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} / \sqrt {n _ {0}} \rangle \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \left\| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \right\| + \langle \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)}, \mathbf {p} ^ {\perp} \mathbf {1} _ {n _ {0}} / \sqrt {n _ {0}} \rangle \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \right\| + \sqrt {\frac {n _ {0} - n}{n _ {0}}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in (i) we used the fact  $\langle \mathbf{p}\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle = \langle \mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\mathbf{p}\rangle$  for any projection matrix  $\mathbf{p}$ . thus, we deduce that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \right\| \geq \sqrt {1 - c _ {4} ^ {\prime} / (\gamma_ {0} n _ {0})} - \sqrt {1 - (n / n _ {0})} \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\geq} 1 - c _ {4} ^ {\prime} / (\gamma_ {0} n _ {0}) - (1 - (n / (2 n _ {0})) \\ \stackrel {(i i)} {\geq} c _ {0} - c _ {4} ^ {\prime} / (\gamma_ {0} n _ {0}) \\ \end{array}
$$

where in  $(i)$  we used  $1 - a\leq \sqrt{1 - a}\leq 1 - (a / 2)$  for  $a\in (0,1)$ , and in  $(ii)$ $c_{0} > 0$  is certain small constant. therefore, if  $\gamma_0n_0\geq 2c_4'$ , then  $\| \mathbf{p}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{v}}_s^{(1)}\| \geq c_0 / 2$ . this proves the claim (145).

in order to bound the smallest eigenvalue in (143), we consider two cases.

case 1:  $\gamma_0 n_0 < c_3$ . we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) \geq \lambda_ {\min } \left(\gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) \geq \frac {\gamma_ {> \ell}}{\max \left\{\gamma_ {0} n _ {0} , \gamma_ {1} n _ {0} / d \right\}} \\ \geq \frac {\gamma_ {> \ell}}{\max \{c _ {3} , c (n / d) \}} \geq c. \\ \end{array}
$$

case 2:  $\gamma_0n_0\geq c_3$ . we derive two lower bounds on its variational form. let  $\pmb {z}\in \mathbb{s}^{n_0 - 1}$  by any vector, and we denote  $\pmb {z} = [z_{1},z_{-1}]^{\top}$ . the first lower bound is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \right] \boldsymbol {z} \\ \geq z _ {1} ^ {2} \big \| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \big \| ^ {2} + 2 z _ {1} [ \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} ^ {(1)} ] ^ {\top} \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(2)} \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} + \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} ^ {\top} [ \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(2)} ] ^ {\top} \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\mathbf {v}} ^ {(2)} \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} \boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {z} \\ \geq z _ {1} ^ {2} \left\| \mathbf {p} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {v}} _ {s} ^ {(1)} \right\| ^ {2} - 2 | z _ {1} | \cdot \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| - \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| ^ {2} \\ \geq \left(1 - \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| ^ {2}\right) \cdot c _ {0} ^ {\prime} - 3 \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \|. \tag {146} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the claim (145) in  $(i)$ . the second lower bound is

$$
\begin{array}{l} \boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \right] \boldsymbol {z} \geq \boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top} \left[ \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \right] \boldsymbol {z} \geq \gamma_ {> \ell} \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| ^ {2} \cdot \frac {c}{\gamma_ {1} n _ {0} / d} \\ \geq \frac {c \gamma_ {> \ell} \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| ^ {2} d}{\gamma_ {1} n} \geq c _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {z} _ {- 1} \| ^ {2} \tag {147} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c_{1} > 0$  is certain small constant, and in the last inequality we used our assumption  $n \leq (c_3 + 1)d$ . if  $\| \pmb{z}_{-1}\| \leq \min \{1 / 2,c_0' / 12\}$ , then by the first lower bound (146), we have

$$
\boldsymbol {z} ^ {\top} \left[ \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1} \right] \boldsymbol {z} \geq c _ {0} / 4.
$$

if  $\| z_{-1} \| \geq \min \{1/2, c_0' / 12\}$  instead, then the second lower bound (147) implies that the left-hand side above is lower bounded by a constant. combining the two cases, we obtain

$$
\lambda_ {\min } \left(\widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\mathbf {u}} _ {1} + \gamma_ {> \ell} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\gamma}) ^ {- 1}\right) \geq c.
$$

this proves the desired inequality (143). the remaining proof is similar proof of lemma 16.

# d generalization error for linear model: proof of corollary 3.2

throughout this subsection, let the assumptions of corollary 3.2 hold. denote  $\overline{\lambda} \coloneqq \lambda + v(\sigma)$ . first, we state and prove a lemma. we will use the simple identity

$$
\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} = \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} - \mathbf {a} _ {0}) \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1}, \qquad \mathrm {f o r a l l} \mathbf {a}, \mathbf {a} _ {0} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n \times n}
$$

multiple times.

lemma 17. there exist constants  $c, c > 0$  such that the following holds. with high probability,

$$
\mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \geq \frac {c n}{\bar {\lambda}},
$$

$$
\left\| \gamma_ {0} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \leq \frac {c}{\sqrt {n}}.
$$

proof of lemma 17. since  $\| \pmb{x}\pmb{x}^{\top}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c(n + d)$  with very high probability (by lemma 21), if  $n\leq 16d$  , we have

$$
\mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \big (\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \pmb {x} \pmb {x} ^ {\top} \big) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \geq \frac {n}{\lambda_ {\max} \big (\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {1} \pmb {x} \pmb {x} ^ {\top} / d \big)} \geq \frac {n}{\overline {{\lambda}} + c \gamma_ {1}} \geq \frac {n}{c ^ {\prime} \overline {{\lambda}}}.
$$

where in the last inequality we used  $\overline{\lambda} \geq c$  for a constant  $c > 0$  so  $\overline{\lambda} + c\lambda_1 \leq c'\overline{\lambda}$ . if  $n > 16d$ , we observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \left(\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} - \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} (\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} = - \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {- 1} (\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n}) ^ {- 1} \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \left(\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \\ = - \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{\bar {\lambda} d} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

since  $\lambda_{\min}(\pmb{x}^{\top}\pmb{x}) \geq n/4$  and  $\| \pmb{x}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_n\|^2 \leq 2nd$  with high probability due to lemma 21, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \geq \frac {n}{\bar {\lambda}} - \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{\bar {\lambda} d} \cdot \| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \| ^ {2} \cdot \frac {4 d}{\lambda_ {1} n} \\ \geq \frac {n}{\bar {\lambda}} - \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{\bar {\lambda} d} \cdot 2 n d \cdot \frac {4 d}{\lambda_ {1} n} \geq \frac {n}{\bar {\lambda}} - \frac {8 d}{\bar {\lambda}} \\ \geq \frac {n}{2 \bar {\lambda}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in either case, the first inequality of the lemma holds with high probability. next, we notice that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} - \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \\ = - \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

by re-arranging the equality, we get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| = \left[ 1 + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right] ^ {- 1} \left\| \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \\ \leq \frac {\sqrt {n} / \overline {{\lambda}}}{1 + c \gamma_ {0} n / \overline {{\lambda}}} = \frac {\sqrt {n}}{\overline {{\lambda}} + c \gamma_ {0} n} \leq \frac {c}{\gamma_ {0} \sqrt {n}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

which proves the lemma.

building on this lemma, we prove some useful bounds. for convenience, we define

$$
\mathbf {a} = \bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}, \quad \mathbf {a} _ {0} = \bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}. \tag {148}
$$

lemma 18. we have

$$
\left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (\sqrt {d}), \quad \left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {f} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d), \tag {149a}
$$

$$
\left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d), \quad \left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {f} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(d ^ {3 / 2}\right), \tag {149b}
$$

$$
\left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \left\| \right. _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d), \quad \left. \gamma_ {1} \right\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d). \right. \tag {149c}
$$

proof of lemma 18. in this proof, we will use the bounds on the singular values of  $x$  from lemma 21. to prove the two bounds in (149a), we observe that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} - \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} = \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} \leq \gamma_ {0} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \cdot \left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \frac {c}{\sqrt {n}} \cdot \sqrt {n} \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in  $(i)$  we used lemma 17. so by the triangle inequality, we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \quad \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} \leq \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} + c \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f}.
$$

if  $n / d > 1 + c$  for any constant  $c > 0$ , then by lemma 21 with high probability  $\lambda_{\min}(\boldsymbol{x}^\top \boldsymbol{x}) > c'n$  for certain constant  $c' > 0$ . since  $n > c_0d$ , we also have  $\| \boldsymbol{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq c\sqrt{n}$  and  $\| \boldsymbol{x}\|_f \leq cn$  with high probability. thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} c \sqrt {n} \cdot \frac {d}{\gamma_ {1} n}, \tag {150} \\ \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} \leq \left\| \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} \cdot \left\| \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c n \cdot \frac {d}{\gamma_ {1} n} \leq \frac {c d}{\gamma_ {1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where in  $(i)$  we used  $\| \mathbf{m}_1\mathbf{m}_2\| _f\leq \| \mathbf{m}_1\| _f\cdot \| \mathbf{m}_2\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  for matrices  $\mathbf{m}_1,\mathbf{m}_2$ . if  $n / d\leq 1 + c$  instead, then  $\left\| \mathbf{a}_0^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c\left\| \mathbf{x}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq c\sqrt{n}\leq o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(\sqrt{d})$ , and  $\left\| \mathbf{a}_0^{-1}\mathbf{x}\right\| _f\leq c\left\| \mathbf{x}\right\| _f\leq cn\leq o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(d)$ . in either cases, the two bounds in (149a) are true. to prove the two bounds in (149b), we note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \overset {(i)} {\leq} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \cdot \sqrt {n} \left\| \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (\sqrt {n}) \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(n ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\gamma_ {1} ^ {- 1} d\right) = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\gamma_ {1} ^ {- 1} d\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} \left(\overline {{\lambda}} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\lambda_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {d}{\gamma_ {1}}.
$$

where we used the bound (150) in (i). this leads to  $\gamma_1\| \pmb{x}^\top \pmb{a}^{-1}\pmb{x}\|_{\mathrm{op}} = o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(d)$ . also, the rank of  $\pmb{x}^\top \pmb{a}^{-1}\pmb{x}$  is at most  $d$ , so

$$
\gamma_ {1} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} \leq \gamma_ {1} \sqrt {d} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(d ^ {3 / 2}\right).
$$

to prove the two bounds in (149c), we let the eigenvalue decomposition of  $\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top}$  be  $\boldsymbol{x}\boldsymbol{x}^{\top} = \mathbf{u}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathbf{d}_{\boldsymbol{x}}\mathbf{u}_{\boldsymbol{x}}^{\top}$  and we find

$$
\gamma_ {1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} = \mathbf {u} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \operatorname {d i a g} \left\{\frac {\lambda_ {1} (\boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top})}{\gamma_ {1} \lambda_ {1} (\boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}) / d + \bar {\lambda}}, \dots , \frac {\lambda_ {d} (\boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top})}{\gamma_ {1} \lambda_ {d} (\boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}) / d + \bar {\lambda}} \right\} \mathbf {u} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {\top}.
$$

the diagonal entries are all no larger than  $d$ , so this proves the first bound in (149c). moreover,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} - \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \sqrt {n} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \\ \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d) \cdot \sqrt {n} \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(n ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (d), \\ \end{array}
$$

which proves the second bound.

to study the risk  $r_{\mathsf{prr}}(\overline{\lambda})$  in the linear case, we first notice that  $k^{p}(\boldsymbol{x}_{i},\boldsymbol{x}_{j}) = \gamma_{0} + \frac{\gamma_{1}}{d}\boldsymbol{x}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{x}_{j}$ . thus, we have

$$
\mathbf {k} ^ {p} = \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}, \qquad \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} = \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}
$$

where the second equality is due to  $\mathbb{e}[\pmb{x}\pmb{x}^{\top}] = \mathbf{i}_d$ . now we break down the risk.

$$
r _ {\mathsf {p r r}} (\overline {{\lambda}}) = e _ {\mathrm {b i a s}} ^ {p} (\overline {{\lambda}}) + e _ {\mathrm {v a r}} ^ {p} (\overline {{\lambda}}) + e _ {\mathrm {c r o s s}} ^ {p} (\overline {{\lambda}}).
$$

the expressions for the bias/variance/cross terms are given below.

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {\text {b i a s}} ^ {p} = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ \left(f (\boldsymbol {x}) - \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) ^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f}\right) ^ {2} \right] \\ = \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} \left[ f (\boldsymbol {x}) \mathbf {k} ^ {p} (\cdot , \boldsymbol {x}) ^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f} \right] + \boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {k} ^ {(p, 2)} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {f} \\ = \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \\ + \beta_ {*} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \beta_ {*} =: \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} - 2 i _ {1} ^ {(1)} + i _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {\text {v a r}} ^ {p} = \varepsilon^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \varepsilon =: i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)}, \\ e _ {\mathrm {c r o s s}} ^ {p} = \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \varepsilon^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} - \varepsilon^ {\top} (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) (\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {n} + \mathbf {k} ^ {p}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \\ =: i _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} - i _ {\varepsilon , 1} ^ {(2)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to simplify these expressions, we notice that we can assume  $\beta_{*}$  to be uniformly random on a sphere with given radius  $r\coloneqq \| \pmb{\beta}_{*}\|_{2}$ :  $\beta_{*}\sim \mathrm{unif}(\mathbb{s}^{d - 1}(r))$ . to understand why this is the case, let us denote the risk  $r_{\mathsf{prr}} = r_{\mathsf{prr}}(\pmb {x}_1,\dots ,\pmb {x}_n,\pmb {\beta}_*)$  to emphasize its dependence on  $\pmb{x}$ ,  $\beta_{*}$ . let  $\mathbf{r}\in \mathcal{o}(n)$  be a random orthogonal matrix with uniform (haar) distribution. then,

$$
r _ {\mathsf {p r r}} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n}, \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*}) = r _ {\mathsf {p r r}} (\mathbf {r} \boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \dots , \mathbf {r} \boldsymbol {x} _ {n}, \mathbf {r} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*}) \overset {d} {=} r _ {\mathsf {p r r}} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}, \dots , \boldsymbol {x} _ {n}, \mathbf {r} \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*}).
$$

the first equality follows from straightforward calculation, and the distributional equivalence  $\stackrel{d}{=}$  holds because the distribution of  $\pmb{x}_i$  is orthogonally invariant. considering therefore, without loss of generality,  $\beta_{*} \sim \mathrm{unif}(\mathbb{s}^{d-1}(r))$ , it is easy to further simplify the terms  $e_{\mathrm{bias}}^p, e_{\mathrm{var}}^p, e_{\mathrm{cross}}^p$ . indeed, we expect these terms to concentrate around their expectation with respect to  $\beta_{*}, \varepsilon$ . this is formally stated in the next lemma.

lemma 19 (concentration to the trace). we have

$$
\max  \left\{\left| i _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} \right|, \left| i _ {\varepsilon , 1} ^ {(2)} \right| \right\} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {\tau^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right), \tag {151a}
$$

$$
\left| i _ {1} ^ {(1)} - \frac {\gamma_ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {\tau^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right), \tag {151b}
$$

$$
\left| i _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} - \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {\tau^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right), \tag {151c}
$$

$$
\left. \left| i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} - \sigma_ {\varepsilon} ^ {2} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {\tau^ {2}}{\sqrt {d}}\right). \right. \tag {151d}
$$

to further simplify the terms, we will show the following lemma.

lemma 20. we have

$$
\left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) - \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right), \tag {152a}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {1}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) - \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {3}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right), \tag {152b}
$$

$$
\left| \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) - \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right). \tag {152c}
$$

once these bounded are proved, then by lemma 23 we have

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1}\right) = \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 2} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) = \frac {1}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} \left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 2}\right), \tag {153a}
$$

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {3}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) = \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {3}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 2} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) = \frac {1}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} ^ {2} \left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 2}\right), \tag {153b}
$$

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) = \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {2}} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} \left(\bar {\lambda} \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) = \frac {1}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} \left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 1}\right). \tag {153c}
$$

where we recall  $\gamma := \gamma_{\mathrm{eff}}(\lambda, \sigma) = \overline{\lambda} / \gamma_1$  and denote  $\mathbf{s} = \mathbf{x}^\top \mathbf{x} / d$ . hence, we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {\text {b i a s}} ^ {p} = \| f \| _ {l ^ {2}} ^ {2} - \frac {2 \| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} (\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}) ^ {- 1}\right) + \frac {\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} ^ {2} (\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}) ^ {- 2}\right) + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}) \\ = \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {i} _ {d}\right) - \frac {2 \left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} \left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) + \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} ^ {2} \left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 2}\right) + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}\right) \\ = \frac {\gamma^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\left(\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}\right) ^ {- 2}\right) + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last equality is due to  $(\gamma \mathbf{i}_d + \mathbf{s})^2 - 2\mathbf{s}(\gamma \mathbf{i}_d + \mathbf{s}) - \mathbf{s}^2 = \gamma^2\mathbf{i}_d$ . also,

$$
e _ {\mathrm {v a r}} ^ {p} = \frac {\sigma_ {\varepsilon} ^ {2}}{d} \mathrm {t r} \big (\mathbf {s} (\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}) ^ {- 2} \big) + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}), \qquad e _ {\mathrm {c r o s s}} ^ {p} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}).
$$

this proves that

$$
r _ {\mathsf {p r r}} (\bar {\lambda}) = \frac {\gamma^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left((\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}) ^ {- 2}\right) + \frac {\sigma_ {\varepsilon} ^ {2}}{d} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {s} (\gamma \mathbf {i} _ {d} + \mathbf {s}) ^ {- 2}\right) + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\tau^ {2} / \sqrt {d}\right) \tag {154}
$$

similar to the decomposition of the risk  $r_{\mathsf{prr}}(\bar{\lambda})$  and lemma 20, we can show that  $r_{\mathrm{lin}}(\gamma)$  has a similar variance-bias decomposition and that the components concentrate to their respective trace,

which yield eqs. (25)-(27). combining these with theorem 3.3 and (154), we arrive at the claims in corollary 3.2.

to complete the proof, below we first prove lemma 20 and then lemma 19.

proof of lemma 20. to show the claim (152a), first we notice that

$$
0 \leq \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) = \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{n}\right)
$$

where we used lemma 17. further,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) - \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) \right| = \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) \right| \\ \leq \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \cdot \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \\ \stackrel {(i)} {\leq} \frac {c}{\sqrt {n}} \cdot \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{\overline {{\lambda}} d ^ {2}} \cdot \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \sqrt {n} \\ \leq \frac {c \gamma_ {1}}{\bar {\lambda} d} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where in (i) we used lemma 17 and  $\| \mathbf{a}^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq \overline{\lambda}^{-1}$ , in (ii) we used  $\frac{\gamma_1}{d}\| xx^\top \mathbf{a}_0^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq 1$  from lemma 18. similarly, we have

$$
\left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1}\right) - \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1}\right) \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right)
$$

which proves (152a). next, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{d} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| \leq \frac {1}{d} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{d} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \\ \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {n}{d}\right) \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used lemma 17. also,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{d} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) - \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| \\ = \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {3}} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| \\ = \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {3}} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \right| \\ \leq \frac {1}{d ^ {3}} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \cdot \sqrt {n} \\ \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used lemma 17 and 18 in the last inequality. similarly, we can also prove

$$
\frac {1}{d} \big | \mathrm {t r} \big (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \big) - \mathrm {t r} \big (\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \big) \big | \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big),
$$

which proves (152b). finally, we observe

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) - \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) = \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

thus we get

$$
\frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {2}} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) - \operatorname {t r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x}\right) \right| \leq \frac {\sqrt {n}}{d} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| \cdot \left\| \frac {\gamma_ {1}}{d} \mathbf {a} _ {0} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right).
$$

which proves (152c).

proof of lemma 19. step 1. we will use the variance calculation of quadratic forms from [mm21]: suppose  $\pmb{g}$  is a vector of i.i.d. random variables  $g_{i} \sim \mathbb{p}_{g}$  which have zero mean and variance  $\sigma_{g}^{2}$ , and  $\pmb{h}$  is another vector of i.i.d. random variables  $h_{i} \sim \mathbb{p}_{h}$  with zero mean and variance  $\sigma_{h}^{2}$ , then for matrices  $\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}$ ,

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ \boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {h} \right] = \sigma_ {g} ^ {2} \sigma_ {h} ^ {2} \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} ^ {2}, \quad \operatorname {v a r} \left[ \boldsymbol {g} ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} \boldsymbol {g} \right] = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} b _ {i i} ^ {2} \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ g ^ {4} \right] - 3 \sigma_ {g} ^ {4}\right) + \sigma_ {g} ^ {4} \| \mathbf {b} \| _ {f} ^ {2} + \sigma_ {g} ^ {4} \operatorname {t r} \left(\mathbf {b} ^ {2}\right). \tag {155}
$$

in particular, if  $\mathbf{b}$  is symmetric and  $\mathbb{e}(g_i^4)\leq c\sigma_g^4$ , then the second variance is bounded by  $o(\sigma_g^4)\cdot$ $\| \mathbf{b}\| _f^2$ . in order to apply these identities, we use the gaussian vector  $\pmb {g}\in \mathcal{n}(\mathbf{0},\frac{\|\pmb{\beta}_*\|^2}{d}\mathbf{i}_d)$  as a surrogate for  $\beta_{*}\sim \mathrm{unif}(\| \beta_{*}\| \mathbb{s}^{d - 1})$ , which only incurs small errors since gaussian vector norm concentrates in high dimensions. we use  $\widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},\widetilde{i}_{1}^{(1)},\widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon ,\varepsilon}^{(2)},\widetilde{i}_{1,1}^{(2)},\widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon ,1}^{(2)}$  to denote the surrogate terms after we replace  $\beta_{*}$  by  $\pmb{g}$  in the definitions of  $i_{\varepsilon}^{(1)},i_{1}^{(1)},i_{\varepsilon ,\varepsilon}^{(2)},i_{1,1}^{(2)},i_{\varepsilon ,1}^{(2)}$ . by homogeneity, we assume that  $\| \beta_{*}\| = \sigma_{\varepsilon} = 1$  without loss of generality.

using lemma 18, we have

$$
\mathrm {v a r} \big [ \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} \big ] \leq \frac {c \gamma_ {1}}{d ^ {3}} \big \| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \big \| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big), \qquad \mathrm {v a r} \big [ \widetilde {i} _ {1} ^ {(1)} \big ] \leq \frac {c \gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {4}} \big \| \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \big \| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big).
$$

also, using lemma 18 and lemma 20,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{d} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{d} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{d} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {4} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \\ \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d n ^ {2}}\right) \cdot o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} (n) = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d n}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\frac {1}{d} \big \| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \pmb {x} \pmb {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \pmb {x} \big \| _ {f} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{d ^ {5}} \big \| \gamma_ {1} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \pmb {x} \big \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \big \| \gamma_ {1} \pmb {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \pmb {x} \big \| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big).
$$

combining the two bounds proves

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \big [ \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon , 1} ^ {(2)} \big ] \leq \frac {c}{d} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \big (\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \big) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big).
$$

similar to this, we derive

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {4} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \big) \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \big), \\ \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \pmb {x} \pmb {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{d ^ {4}} \left\| \gamma_ {1} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \pmb {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {1} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \pmb {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big), \\ \frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \left\| \gamma_ {0} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {4} \leq \frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {0} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \right\| ^ {4} \leq o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{n ^ {2}} \big), \\ \frac {1}{d ^ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{d ^ {6}} \left\| \gamma_ {1} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \gamma_ {1} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big). \\ \end{array}
$$

combining these two bounds give

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} \right] \leq c \| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right),
$$

$$
\operatorname {v a r} \left[ \widetilde {i} _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} \right] \leq \frac {c}{d ^ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} ^ {- 1} \left(\gamma_ {0} ^ {2} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \mathbf {1} _ {n} ^ {\top} + \frac {\gamma_ {1} ^ {2}}{d ^ {2}} \boldsymbol {x} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {a} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \left(\frac {1}{d}\right).
$$

step 2. finally, to prove the desired results, we connect  $\widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}, \widetilde{i}_{1}^{(1)}, \widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon}^{(2)}, \widetilde{i}_{1,1}^{(2)}, \widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon, 1}^{(2)}$  back to  $i_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}, i_{1}^{(1)}, i_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon}^{(2)}, i_{1,1}^{(2)}, i_{\varepsilon, 1}^{(2)}$ . by definition,  $\beta_*$  and  $\| \beta_* \| g / \| g \|$  have the same distribution, so there is no loss of generality by writing

$$
i _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} = \frac {\| \pmb {\beta} _ {*} \|}{\| \pmb {g} \|} \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)}, i _ {1} ^ {(1)} = \frac {\| \pmb {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\| \pmb {g} \| ^ {2}} \widetilde {i} _ {1} ^ {(1)}, i _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} = \frac {\| \pmb {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\| \pmb {g} \| ^ {2}} \widetilde {i} _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)}, i _ {\varepsilon , 1} ^ {(2)} = \frac {\| \pmb {\beta} _ {*} \|}{\| \pmb {g} \|} \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon , 1} ^ {(2)}.
$$

and also  $\widetilde{i}_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon}^{(2)} = i_{\varepsilon, \varepsilon}^{(2)}$  because there is no  $\beta_*$  involved. by concentration of gaussian vector norms [ver10, vh14], we have  $\| \pmb{g} \| / \| \pmb{\beta}_* \| = 1 + o_{d, \mathbb{p}}(d^{-1/2})$ . we observe that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\pmb {\varepsilon}, \pmb {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} ] = 0, \quad \mathrm {v a r} _ {\pmb {\varepsilon}, \pmb {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} ] = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big) \qquad \rightarrow \qquad \big | \widetilde {i} _ {\varepsilon} ^ {(1)} \big | = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big)
$$

by chebyshev's inequality, and thus  $\left|i_{\varepsilon}^{(1)}\right| = o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(d^{-1 / 2})$ . the same argument applies to the term  $i_{\varepsilon ,1}^{(2)}$  which leads to the bound  $\left|i_{\varepsilon ,1}^{(2)}\right| = o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(d^{-1 / 2})$ . also, by a similar argument,

$$
\frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{\left\| \boldsymbol {g} \right\| ^ {2}} \big | \widetilde {i} _ {1} ^ {(1)} - \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {1} ^ {(1)} ] \big | = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big), \quad \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \right\| ^ {2}}{\left\| \boldsymbol {g} \right\| ^ {2}} \big | \widetilde {i} _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} - \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} ] \big | = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{d} \big),
$$

$$
\left| i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} - \mathbb {e} _ {\varepsilon} [ i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} ] \right| = o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big).
$$

therefore, we deduce

$$
i _ {1} ^ {(1)} = \frac {\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {g} \| ^ {2}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {1} ^ {(1)} ] + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big), \qquad i _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} = \frac {\| \boldsymbol {\beta} _ {*} \| ^ {2}}{\| \boldsymbol {g} \| ^ {2}} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {g}} [ \widetilde {i} _ {1, 1} ^ {(2)} ] + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big),
$$

$$
i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} = \mathbb {e} _ {\pmb {\varepsilon}} [ i _ {\varepsilon , \varepsilon} ^ {(2)} ] + o _ {d, \mathbb {p}} \big (\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \big).
$$

note that  $\| \pmb{g}\| ^2 /\| \pmb{\beta}_*\| ^2 = 1 + o_{d,\mathbb{p}}(d^{-1 / 2})$  and  $\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{g}}[\widetilde{i}_{1}^{(1)}],\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{g}}[\widetilde{i}_{1,1}^{(2)}],\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{\varepsilon}}[i_{\varepsilon ,\varepsilon}^{(2)}]$  are exactly the trace quantities in the statement of lemma 19. also note that  $|\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{g}}[\widetilde{i}_{1}^{(1)}]|,|\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{g}}[\widetilde{i}_{1,1}^{(2)}]|,|\mathbb{e}_{\pmb{\varepsilon}}[i_{\varepsilon ,\varepsilon}^{(2)}]|$  are bounded by a constant due to lemma 20 and eqs. (153a)-(153c). this completes the proof.

![](images/8dd5ebc5bc6541ee622393e6bc9985d7c423f9733657e062c9724219c130059d.jpg)

# e supporting lemmas and proofs

# e.1 basic lemmas

lemma 21. fix any constant  $\varepsilon_0\in (0,0.1)$ . with very high probability,  $\| x\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq (1 + \varepsilon_0)(\sqrt{n} +\sqrt{d})$  and  $\sigma_{\min}(\pmb {x})\geq (\sqrt{n} -(1 + \varepsilon_0)\sqrt{d}) / (1 + \varepsilon_0)$ , and  $\| \pmb{x}^{\top}\pmb{1}_n\| \leq (1 + \varepsilon_0)\sqrt{nd}$

proof of lemma 21. let  $\widetilde{\pmb{x}} = [\widetilde{\pmb{x}}_1,\dots ,\widetilde{\pmb{x}}_n]^\top \in \mathbb{r}^{n\times d}$  be a matrix of i.i.d. standard normal variable, and  $\widetilde{\mathbf{d}}_{\pmb{x}} = d^{-1 / 2}\mathrm{diag}\{\| \widetilde{\pmb{x}}_1\| ,\ldots ,\| \widetilde{\pmb{x}}_n\| \}$ . by [ver10][corollary 5.35],  $\| \widetilde{\pmb{x}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq (1 + \varepsilon_0 / 2)(\sqrt{n} +$

$\sqrt{d})$  with probability at least  $1 - 2\exp (-c(n + d))$ . by the subgaussian concentration,  $d^{-1 / 2}\| \widetilde{\boldsymbol{x}}_i\| \in (1 - \varepsilon_0 / 10,1 + \varepsilon_0 / 10)$  with probability at least  $1 - 2\exp (-cd)$ . taking the union bound, we deduce that with probability at least  $1 - cn\exp (-cd)$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {x} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} / \lambda_ {\min } (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}) <   (1 + \varepsilon_ {0}) (\sqrt {n} + \sqrt {d}), \\ \sigma_ {\min} (\boldsymbol {x}) = \sigma_ {\min} \bigl (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} \bigr) \geq \sigma_ {\min} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}}) / \lambda_ {\max} (\widetilde {\mathbf {d}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}) > (\sqrt {n} - (1 + \varepsilon_ {0}) \sqrt {d}) / (1 + \varepsilon_ {0}), \\ \| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \| = \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mathbf {d}}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {- 1} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \| \leq \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {x}} ^ {\top} \mathbf {1} _ {n} \| / \lambda_ {\min } (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {\mathbf {d}}} _ {\boldsymbol {x}}) <   (1 + \varepsilon_ {0}) \sqrt {n d}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used concentration of gaussian vector norms [ver10, vh14] in the last inequality. this finishes the proof.

lemma 22. let  $a > 0$  be any constant,  $\pmb{x}^{\prime} \in \mathbb{s}^{d - 1}(\sqrt{d})$  be fixed, and  $\pmb{x} \sim \mathrm{unif}(\mathbb{s}^{d - 1}(\sqrt{d}))$ . then  $|\langle \pmb{x}, \pmb{x}^{\prime} \rangle| \leq a\sqrt{d}\log d$  holds with very high probability.

proof of lemma 22. by orthogonal invariance, we assume without loss of generality that  $\boldsymbol{x}' = \sqrt{d}\boldsymbol{e}_1$ . it suffices to prove that  $|\langle \boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{e}_1\rangle| \leq a\log d$  holds with very high probability. note that  $\boldsymbol{x} \stackrel{d}{=} \sqrt{d}\boldsymbol{g}/\|\boldsymbol{g}\|$  where  $\boldsymbol{g} \sim \mathcal{n}(0,\mathbf{i}_d)$ . from basic concentration results [ver10], we know  $\| \boldsymbol{g} \| / \sqrt{d} \geq 1/2$  and  $|\langle \boldsymbol{x},\boldsymbol{e}_1\rangle| \leq a\log d/2$  with very high probability. this then proves the desired result.

lemma 23. let  $\mathbf{b} \in \mathbb{r}^{m \times q}$  be a matrix and  $a > 0$ . then,

$$
\left(\mathbf {b} \mathbf {b} ^ {\top} + a \mathbf {i} _ {m}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {b} = \mathbf {b} \left(\mathbf {b} ^ {\top} \mathbf {b} + a \mathbf {i} _ {q}\right) ^ {- 1} \tag {156}
$$

this simple lemma is straightforward to check (left-multiplying  $\left(\mathbf{bb}^{\top} + a\mathbf{i}_m\right)^{-1}$  and right-multiplying  $\left(\mathbf{b}^{\top}\mathbf{b} + a\mathbf{i}_q\right)^{-1}$ ).

lemma 24. let  $\mathcal{h}$  be an  $l^2$  space and  $\langle \cdot, \cdot \rangle_{\mathcal{h}}$  be the associated inner product. if  $f, f_m, g, g_m \in \mathcal{h}$  for  $m \geq 1$ , and  $f_m \xrightarrow{l^2} f$ ,  $g_m \xrightarrow{l^2} g$ , then  $\langle f_m, g_m \rangle_{\mathcal{h}} \xrightarrow{m \to \infty} \langle f, g \rangle_{\mathcal{h}}$ .

this is a simple convergence result of  $l^2$  spaces.

# e.2 an operator norm bound

recall that  $q_k^{(d)}$  is the degree- $k$  gegenbauer polynomial in  $d$  dimensions. the next lemma states that when the degree is high, the gegenbauer inner-product kernels are very close to the identity matrices. throughout this subsection, we assume that  $d^{\ell + 1} \geq n$ .

proposition e.1. denote  $q_{k} = (q_{k}^{(d)}(\langle \pmb{x}_{i},\pmb{x}_{j}\rangle))_{i,j\leq n}$ . then, there exists a constant  $c > 0$  such that with very high probability,

$$
\sup  _ {k > \ell} \left\| \boldsymbol {q} _ {k} - \mathbf {i} _ {n} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c \sqrt {\frac {n (\log d) ^ {c}}{d ^ {\ell + 1}}}.
$$
